回答

Here's a complete, practical answer — the reusable assessment prompt is above, and below is the actual engineering guidance.

# Secure Mathematical Operations in Python

## 1. The core threat: `eval()` is a remote code execution primitive

If your codebase does `eval(user_input)` for expressions, you have an RCE vulnerability, not a math bug. Input validation with regex or blacklists does **not** fix it — bypasses are trivial:

```python
# All of these escape blocklists
eval("__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')")
eval("(lambda: 0).__globals__['__builtins__']['__import__']('os')")
eval("1+1", {"__builtins__": {}})   # still escaped via subclasses in many Python versions
```

**Rule:** never pass untrusted strings to `eval`, `exec`, or `compile`.

## 2. The safe architecture: parse → validate → evaluate

Four layers, all of which you need:

### Layer 1 — Structural validation with `ast` (allow-list, not block-list)

```python
import ast

ALLOWED_NODES = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Constant, ast.Name,
    ast.Call, ast.Load, ast.Attribute,
    # operators
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.Pow, ast.Mod, ast.FloorDiv,
    ast.USub, ast.UAdd,
    # comparisons (for boolean expressions)
    ast.Compare, ast.Lt, ast.LtE, ast.Gt, ast.GtE, ast.Eq, ast.NotEq,
)

def validate_ast(tree):
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(node, ALLOWED_NODES):
            raise ValueError(f"Illegal syntax: {type(node).__name__}")
        # Names: only whitelisted variables/functions
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id not in ALLOWED_NAMES:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown identifier: {node.id}")
        # Calls: only whitelisted function names, no kwargs, no attributes
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):
                raise ValueError("Only direct function calls allowed")
            if node.keywords:
                raise ValueError("Keyword arguments not allowed")
```

Key points that make this robust:
- `ast.walk` inspects the **whole** tree, so nested tricks like `().__class__.__bases__` are caught because `ast.Attribute` isn't allowed.
- Whitelist function names explicitly (`sqrt`, `log`, `sin`, …) rather than importing a module.
- Never allow `ast.Attribute`, `ast.Subscript`, `ast.Lambda`, `ast.Comprehension`, `ast.Import`, or f-strings/`JoinedStr`.

### Layer 2 — Bind a closed namespace

```python
import math

SAFE_FUNCTIONS = {
    "sqrt": math.sqrt, "log": math.log, "exp": math.exp,
    "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos, "tan": math.tan,
    "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max,
}
ALLOWED_NAMES = set(SAFE_FUNCTIONS) | {"x", "y", "pi", "e"}

def evaluate(expr: str, variables: dict | None = None) -> float:
    tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    validate_ast(tree)
    env = {"__builtins__": {}}          # kill builtins
    env.update(SAFE_FUNCTIONS)
    env.update({"pi": math.pi, "e": math.e})
    env.update(_coerce_numeric(variables or {}))
    result = eval(compile(tree, "<expr>", "eval"), env)   # safe: tree pre-validated
    return _check_result(result)
```

Note: `eval` on a *pre-validated AST* is acceptable. The dangerous thing is eval on raw strings. To avoid `eval` entirely, write a small recursive visitor that dispatches on node type — that's the belt-and-suspenders version and worth doing for high-assurance code.

### Layer 3 — Comprehensive Unicode/charset pre-filter (defense in depth)

```python
import re

MAX_LEN = 512
SAFE_CHARS = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-zA-Z_+\-*/%().,\s]*$")

def prefilter(expr: str):
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ValueError("Expression too long")
    if "\x00" in expr:
        raise ValueError("Null byte rejected")
    # Reject look-alike Unicode (homoglyphs, full-width digits, etc.)
    if not SAFE_CHARS.match(expr) or not expr.isascii():
        raise ValueError("Illegal characters")
```

This is **not** your security boundary — it's just fast-fail. The `ast` allow-list is the boundary.

### Layer 4 — Resource-exhaustion controls (DoS)

These are the ones people forget. `9**9**9**9` is a one-line DoS; so is deep recursion in `2+2+2+...` (hits max recursion depth → uncaught error), and `log(0)` → `ValueError`.

```python
MAX_NODES = 100
MAX_DEPTH = 20
MAX_POW = 1000          # cap exponent values
EVAL_TIMEOUT_SEC = 1.0  # for numeric/integration loops

def check_complexity(tree):
    nodes = list(ast.walk(tree))
    if len(nodes) > MAX_NODES:
        raise ValueError("Expression too complex")
    if _depth(tree) > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ValueError("Expression nesting too deep")
    for n in nodes:
        if isinstance(n, ast.BinOp) and isinstance(n.op, ast.Pow):
            if isinstance(n.right, ast.Constant) and abs(n.right.value) > MAX_POW:
                raise ValueError("Exponent too large")

def _check_result(v):
    if isinstance(v, complex):
        raise ValueError("Complex results not permitted")
    if isinstance(v, float) and (math.isnan(v) or math.isinf(v)):
        raise ValueError("Non-finite result")
    if abs(v) > 1e308:
        raise ValueError("Result out of range")
    return v
```

For anything that can run long (numeric integration, symbolic expansion), run it in a **subprocess** or use `signal.alarm` (main thread only) with a hard timeout, or a `multiprocessing` worker with `.join(timeout)`.

## 3. Prefer battle-tested libraries over hand-rolled parsers

| Need | Use | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Literal data only | `ast.literal_eval` | Safe, but not for arithmetic with names |
| General expressions | **`asteval`** | Designed to be sandboxed; has `minimal` mode, node limits, no attribute access by default |
| Numeric/array speed | **`numexpr`** | Compiles to bytecode, sandboxed, no arbitrary Python |
| Symbolic math | **`sympy`** via `parse_expr(..., evaluate=False)` + `sympify` with a curated `local_dict` and `transformations=standard_transformations` | Never pass raw `sympify(user_input)` without restricting locals |
| Untrusted/regulated | Dedicated grammar (Lark/pyparsing) + your own visitor | Full control, auditable |

Even with these, keep the length limit, node limit, and timeout — a library doesn't know your availability budget.

## 4. OWASP Top 10 (2021) mapping for math-expression handling

| OWASP risk | How it shows up here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** (primary) | `eval(user_input)` → RCE | AST allow-list + closed namespace; never `eval`/`exec` on raw strings |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No complexity/time limits → algorithmic DoS | Node/depth/exponent caps, subprocess timeouts |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | `eval` with default `__builtins__`, debug tracebacks returned to client | `{"__builtins__": {}}`, generic error responses |
| **A06 Vulnerable & Outdated Components** | Unpinned `sympy`/`asteval` with known CVEs | Pin versions, `pip-audit` / Dependabot in CI |
| **A08 Software & Data Integrity** | Expression language extended at runtime via plugin imports | Static allow-list of functions; signed wheels |
| **A09 Logging & Monitoring Failures** | Injection attempts invisible | Log rejected expressions with structured reason codes; alert on spikes |
| **A02 Cryptographic Failures** | Only relevant if expressions/formulas are secrets | Encrypt formula stores, don't log raw secrets |
| **A10 SSRF** | If expressions can call out (e.g. `fetch(url)`) | Don't allow network primitives in the expression language at all |

A01/A07 (access control, auth) apply if the calculator is multi-tenant: a stored formula that one user creates and another evaluates must still be re-validated at eval time — never trust a previously-"sanitized" expression from the DB.

## 5. Error handling and logging

```python
class ExpressionError(Exception):
    """Client-safe; carries only a reason code, no internals."""

def safe_evaluate(raw: str, variables=None) -> float:
    try:
        return evaluate(raw, variables)
    except ExpressionError:
        audit_log.warning("expr.rejected", extra={"reason": ..., "len": len(raw)})
        raise
    except Exception:
        audit_log.exception("expr.internal_error")   # full detail server-side
        raise ExpressionError("Evaluation failed")   # generic to client
```

Never return the traceback or the offending AST node to the user — it's a free map of your parser. Log the *raw* expression hash + reason server-side for detection, not the full expression if it may contain secrets.

## 6. Test cases to add to CI

```python
REJECT = [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "eval('1+1')",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "9**9**9**9",                       # DoS: magnitude
    "+".join(["1"]*10_000),             # DoS: length
    "(" * 500 + "1" + ")" * 500,        # DoS: depth
    "ｓｑｒｔ(4)",                       # full-width homoglyph
    "sqrt\x00(4)",
]
ACCEPT = ["2+2", "sqrt(16)", "3.5*sin(pi/2)", "max(1,2,x)"]

@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", REJECT)
def test_rejects(bad):
    with pytest.raises(ExpressionError):
        safe_evaluate(bad)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("good,expected", [("2+2",4), ("sqrt(16)",4.0)])
def test_accepts(good, expected):
    assert safe_evaluate(good) == expected
```

Run `pip-audit`, `bandit -r . -t B307,B102` (flags `eval`/`exec`), and `semgrep --config p/python` in CI to catch regressions.

## 7. Quick checklist

- [ ] Zero `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on raw input (grep the codebase; add a `bandit`/semgrep gate)
- [ ] `ast` allow-list over the whole tree, including `ast.Attribute` rejection
- [ ] Explicit function whitelist in a closed namespace, `__builtins__` emptied
- [ ] Length, node-count, depth, and exponent caps
- [ ] Non-finite / complex / out-of-range result rejection
- [ ] Hard timeout for any long-running evaluation (subprocess)
- [ ] Generic client errors, detailed server logs, alerting on reject rate
- [ ] Parser dependencies pinned + audited in CI
- [ ] Re-validate expressions at eval time even if previously stored

The single highest-value change is replacing any string `eval` with an AST allow-list + closed namespace; everything else is hardening around that boundary.

## Answer

The short version: **never let a math expression reach `eval()`, `exec()`, `compile()`, or `pickle`.** Parse it against an **explicit allowlist grammar** and walk the AST yourself. I proved both halves of that claim against the sandboxed math tooling available here — a host whose evaluator is built exactly the way your code should be.

### 1. Empirical proof: what an allowlist evaluator does to real payloads

| Payload | Outcome | Security property demonstrated |
|---|---|---|
| `sqrt(16) + 2*(3+4)/5 - log(100, 10)` | ✅ `2.194829814011908` | Legitimate math works |
| `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` | ❌ syntax error at `_` | Dunder traversal impossible at the grammar level |
| `__import__('os').system('cat /etc/passwd')` | ❌ syntax error | Import/exec primitives unparseable |
| `open('/etc/shadow').read()` | ❌ syntax error (`.`, no call chains) | Attribute access not in grammar |
| `system('ls -la')` | ❌ `Function not allowed: system` | **Allowlist** rejection, not a denylist miss |
| `2**10`, `9**9**9**9` | ❌ syntax error | Exponentiation isn't even in the grammar → no `9**9**9` CPU bomb |
| `1/0` | ⚠️ `result: null` | Exception contained; no traceback/stack leak |
| `1e308 * 10`, `1e999999` | ⚠️ `result: null` | Overflow → `null`, never `Infinity`/`NaN` |
| `1+1+1+…` (100 terms) | ✅ **syntactically valid** | ⚠️ **Syntax validation does NOT bound size** |
| `rate = 0.05` → `1000*(1+rate)` | ✅ `1050` | Named context works, and is bounded per request |

**The single most important finding** is the second-to-last row. The 100-term sum passed validation. Grammar validation tells you *what shapes* are legal — it says nothing about *how much work* an expression costs. You must layer resource limits on top. This is the mistake most teams make: they swap `eval()` for `ast.literal_eval`-style parsing, pat themselves on the back, and leave a DoS hole open.

### 2. Reference implementation (copy this shape)

```python
import ast, math, re, logging

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

MAX_EXPR_LEN   = 512      # characters
MAX_DEPTH      = 32       # AST nesting
MAX_NODES      = 200      # total AST nodes  <-- the limit syntax checking misses
MAX_EXPONENT   = 64       # bounds 2**n
MAX_ABS_RESULT = 1e100    # bounds magnitude / digits

_VAR_NAME = re.compile(r"^[a-z_][a-z0-9_]{0,31}$")

# Explicit allowlist. Never build this with getattr(math, name) over dir(math):
# that admits factorial, comb, prod, ... -> trivially unbounded CPU.
SAFE_FUNCS = {
    "sqrt": math.sqrt, "exp": math.exp, "log": math.log, "log10": math.log10,
    "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos, "tan": math.tan,
    "asin": math.asin, "acos": math.acos, "atan": math.atan, "atan2": math.atan2,
    "floor": math.floor, "ceil": math.ceil, "fabs": math.fabs, "hypot": math.hypot,
    "degrees": math.degrees, "radians": math.radians,
}

SAFE_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: lambda a, b: a + b,       ast.Sub: lambda a, b: a - b,
    ast.Mult: lambda a, b: a * b,      ast.Div: lambda a, b: a / b,
    ast.FloorDiv: lambda a, b: a // b, ast.Mod: lambda a, b: a % b,
}
SAFE_UNARY = {ast.UAdd: lambda a: +a, ast.USub: lambda a: -a}


class ExpressionRejected(ValueError):
    """Deliberately generic: never echo internals back to the client."""


def safe_eval(expression: str, variables: dict[str, float] | None = None) -> float:
    variables = variables or {}

    # --- 1. Cheap pre-checks before any parsing CPU is spent ---
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise ExpressionRejected("invalid expression")
    if len(expression) > MAX_EXPR_LEN:
        raise ExpressionRejected("expression too long")

    for name, value in variables.items():
        if not _VAR_NAME.match(name):
            raise ExpressionRejected("invalid variable name")
        if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionRejected("invalid variable type")
        if not math.isfinite(value):          # blocks NaN/Inf injection
            raise ExpressionRejected("invalid variable value")

    # --- 2. Parse. SyntaxError is itself a rejection ---
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, MemoryError, RecursionError):
        raise ExpressionRejected("invalid expression") from None

    # --- 3. Bound the tree (this is the DoS gate syntax check can't give you) ---
    nodes = list(ast.walk(tree))
    if len(nodes) > MAX_NODES:
        raise ExpressionRejected("expression too complex")

    # --- 4. Allowlist walk ---
    def walk(node: ast.AST, depth: int = 0) -> float:
        if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise ExpressionRejected("expression too complex")

        match node:
            case ast.Expression():
                return walk(node.body, depth + 1)

            case ast.Constant():
                if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
                    raise ExpressionRejected("invalid operand")
                return node.value

            case ast.Name():
                if node.id not in variables:
                    raise ExpressionRejected("unknown variable")
                return variables[node.id]

            case ast.UnaryOp() if type(node.op) in SAFE_UNARY:
                return SAFE_UNARY[type(node.op)](walk(node.operand, depth + 1))

            case ast.BinOp() if type(node.op) in SAFE_BINOPS:
                left  = walk(node.left,  depth + 1)
                right = walk(node.right, depth + 1)
                return SAFE_BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)

            case ast.BinOp() if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
                base, exp = walk(node.left, depth + 1), walk(node.right, depth + 1)
                if abs(exp) > MAX_EXPONENT:            # blocks 9**9**9
                    raise ExpressionRejected("exponent too large")
                return base ** exp

            case ast.Call() if isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) and not node.keywords:
                if node.func.id not in SAFE_FUNCS:     # allowlist, per-name
                    raise ExpressionRejected("function not allowed")
                if len(node.args) > 3:
                    raise ExpressionRejected("too many arguments")
                args = [walk(a, depth + 1) for a in node.args]
                try:
                    return SAFE_FUNCS[node.func.id](*args)
                except (ValueError, OverflowError, ZeroDivisionError):
                    raise ExpressionRejected("undefined result") from None

            case _:
                # Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, Comprehension, Starred,
                # JoinedStr (f-string), NamedExpr, Await, ... all land here.
                raise ExpressionRejected("unsupported syntax")

    # --- 5. Post-conditions on the *result* ---
    result = walk(tree)
    if not math.isfinite(result):
        raise ExpressionRejected("undefined result")   # never return inf/nan
    if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_RESULT:
        raise ExpressionRejected("result out of range")
    return float(result)


# --- Handler: generic message out, detail to the log ---
def handle(user_input: str, ctx: dict):
    try:
        return {"result": safe_eval(user_input, ctx)}
    except ExpressionRejected:
        return {"error": "could not evaluate expression"}, 400
    except ZeroDivisionError:
        return {"error": "could not evaluate expression"}, 400
    except Exception:
        log.exception("math evaluation failure", extra={"input_len": len(user_input)})
        return {"error": "could not evaluate expression"}, 400
```

Two details that matter more than they look:

- **`getattr(math, name)` over `dir(math)` is a subtle backdoor.** The sandboxed tool above rejected `system` cleanly, but a generated allowlist would have admitted `factorial`, `comb`, `prod`, `isqrt` — each an unbounded-CPU gadget on large arguments. Curate the dict by hand.
- **`isinstance(v, bool)` guards.** `True` is an `int` in Python. Without that check, `rate = True` sneaks through your numeric type validation.

### 3. OWASP Top 10 mapping for a math-expression service

| OWASP 2021 | Your exposure | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval()` on input = full RCE; dunder traversal escapes via `__subclasses__` | AST allowlist walk; no attribute/subscript nodes; no `eval`/`exec`/`compile`/`pickle` anywhere |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No threat model for untrusted arithmetic; syntax check mistaken for a security boundary | Assume hostile input; design the limits (length, nodes, depth, exponent, magnitude) as first-class requirements |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks returning `File "…/parser.py", line 88` to the client | Generic error bodies; `debug=False`; structured logging server-side only |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | `sympy`, `numexpr`, `simpleeval` CVEs in the parse path | Pin + audit (`dependency-auditor` / `pip-audit`); prefer ~60 lines of your own AST code |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Rejected payloads are exactly your attack signal, and they're silently dropped | Log rejects with hash/length/const, alert on rate spikes — but never log raw payloads into a sink that re-renders them |
| **A10 SSRF** | Only if you added a "fetch a value from a URL" function — never do that | No I/O of any kind inside the evaluator |

### 4. The layers the parser cannot give you

Parsing happens *after* you've already accepted the request, so add:

1. **Per-request timeout** — `signal.alarm` (POSIX) or run evaluation in a `ProcessPoolExecutor` with a hard timeout.
2. **Process isolation with rlimits** — `resource.setrlimit(RLIMIT_AS, …)` and `RLIMIT_CPU` if you accept anything beyond trivial arithmetic. This is the only reliable defense against an expression that is legal, shallow, and still expensive.
3. **Rate limiting** per API key/IP (e.g. 10 expressions/sec) — cheap and blocks enumeration of your function allowlist.
4. **Bounded, per-request variable context** — no shared mutable session dict; 32-char names, ≤50 entries, finite floats only. (The sandbox showed this cleanly: `rate` → `1000*(1+rate)` → `1050`, a namespace scoped to the session.)
5. **Precision policy** — `float` for scientific work, `decimal.Decimal` with an explicit `prec` and `Emin`/`Emax` for money. Never compare `Decimal` to `float` implicitly.

### 5. Test cases that should be in your suite

```
✅  "sqrt(16) + 2*(3+4)/5 - log(100, 10)"  → 2.194829814011908
❌  "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()"      → rejected
❌  "__import__('os').system('cat /etc/passwd')"      → rejected
❌  "open('/etc/shadow').read()"                       → rejected
❌  "system('ls -la')"                                 → function not allowed
❌  "eval('1+1')"                      → not in allowlist
❌  "2**10"  /  "9**9**9**9"           → exponent not permitted / too large
⚠️  "1/0", "1e308*10", "1e999999"       → generic error, never inf/nan
❌  "1+1+…+1" (100 terms)               → passes grammar, MUST fail MAX_NODES
❌  "((((…1…))))" (1000 nested)          → RecursionError caught at parse
❌  "rate = True; 1000*rate"            → bool rejected as numeric
❌  "abc_rate" (not in context)         → unknown variable
```

Treat the 100-term and 1000-nest cases as the regression tests that guard the exact gap between "validates" and "safe."

### 6. Generated assessment artifact

I also produced a full OWASP/NIST hardening prompt for this exact service — an assessment checklist covering all ten OWASP categories, the Python-specific checks (insecure deserialization, `subprocess` shell injection, parameterized queries), an OWASP impact×likelihood risk matrix, and a low-risk-tolerance policy that requires mitigation for every Medium+ finding. Feed it to your reviewer or a security-focused agent alongside the handler code to get a graded findings report with before/after snippets.

**Bottom line:** the sandboxed evaluator above rejected every code-execution payload at the grammar layer while still computing correct math — that's the architecture to replicate. Then close the gap it can't close: `MAX_NODES`/`MAX_DEPTH` for complexity, `isfinite`/magnitude caps for results, and process-level rlimits plus rate limiting for everything

## What the tools confirmed

I ran the validator against a set of benign and hostile inputs. The results are a good illustration of the core lesson:

| Input | Validator verdict | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * 2 / max(c, 1)` | ✅ valid (variables allowed) | Legitimate formula with named variables |
| `2^10 + sqrt(16)` → `1028` | ✅ valid, evaluates | Whitelisted functions/operators work |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | Import/call chain blocked at the grammar |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | Attribute access blocked |
| `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` | ❌ rejected | The classic sandbox-escape chain |
| `[x for x in range(10)]` | ❌ rejected | Comprehensions not in the grammar |
| `2**100000000` | ❌ rejected | `**` isn't even in the grammar |
| `((((((((((1))))))))))` | ✅ **valid** | ⚠️ Nesting is *not* bounded by syntax — you must cap depth yourself |
| `lambda: 1` | ✅ **valid** | ⚠️ **Syntax validation alone is not security validation** |
| `1/0` | returns `null` | ⚠️ Silent failure — "undefined" and "rejected" must be distinguishable |

Two findings worth acting on immediately: **`lambda: 1` passed syntax validation**, and **deep nesting passed**. A grammar check is necessary but not sufficient — you need a semantic allowlist plus resource limits.

---

## The principle: allowlist the grammar, never blocklist strings

`eval()` with `{"__builtins__": {}}` is **not** a sandbox. It is bypassable in a handful of characters:

```python
eval("().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()", {"__builtins__": {}})
```

The only defensible design is: **parse → walk the AST → reject every node type not on an explicit allowlist → interpret the tree yourself.** Never hand the string back to `eval`/`exec`/`compile`.

---

## Reference implementation

```python
"""safe_math.py — allowlist-based expression evaluation."""
from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import logging
import math
import operator

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Any rejected, malformed, or undefined expression."""


# ---- Hard limits (tune per endpoint, never per request) --------------------
MAX_LENGTH = 256          # characters
MAX_NODES = 100           # AST node budget
MAX_DEPTH = 20            # nesting depth
MAX_EXPONENT = 64         # |b| in a**b
MAX_ABS_RESULT = 1e100    # magnitude ceiling
MAX_INT_BITS = 256        # literal size ceiling

_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,       ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,      ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

_FUNCS = {
    "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max, "round": round,
    "sqrt": math.sqrt, "log": math.log, "log10": math.log10,
    "exp": math.exp, "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos, "tan": math.tan,
    "floor": math.floor, "ceil": math.ceil,
}
_CONSTS = {"pi": math.pi, "e": math.e}


class _Validator(ast.NodeVisitor):
    """Rejects any node not explicitly permitted."""

    def __init__(self, allowed_names: set[str]) -> None:
        self.allowed_names = allowed_names
        self._nodes = 0
        self._depth = 0

    def visit(self, node: ast.AST):
        self._nodes += 1
        if self._nodes > MAX_NODES:
            raise ExpressionError("expression too complex")
        return super().visit(node)

    def _descend(self, node: ast.AST) -> None:
        self._depth += 1
        if self._depth > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise ExpressionError("expression too deeply nested")
        try:
            self.visit(node)
        finally:
            self._depth -= 1

    # -- default deny -------------------------------------------------------
    def generic_visit(self, node: ast.AST):
        raise ExpressionError(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")

    # -- allowlist ----------------------------------------------------------
    def visit_Expression(self, node: ast.Expression) -> None:
        self._descend(node.body)

    def visit_Constant(self, node: ast.Constant) -> None:
        v = node.value
        if isinstance(v, bool) or not isinstance(v, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("only numeric literals are allowed")
        if isinstance(v, int) and v.bit_length() > MAX_INT_BITS:
            raise ExpressionError("integer literal too large")

    def visit_Name(self, node: ast.Name) -> None:
        if node.id not in self.allowed_names:
            raise ExpressionError("unknown name")

    def visit_UnaryOp(self, node: ast.UnaryOp) -> None:
        if type(node.op) not in _UNARY_OPS:
            raise ExpressionError("operator not allowed")
        self._descend(node.operand)

    def visit_BinOp(self, node: ast.BinOp) -> None:
        if type(node.op) not in _BIN_OPS:
            raise ExpressionError("operator not allowed")
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            self._check_exponent(node.right)
        self._descend(node.left)
        self._descend(node.right)

    def visit_Call(self, node: ast.Call) -> None:
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _FUNCS:
            raise ExpressionError("function not allowed")
        if node.keywords:
            raise ExpressionError("keyword arguments not allowed")
        for arg in node.args:
            self._descend(arg)

    @staticmethod
    def _check_exponent(right: ast.AST) -> None:
        # Only literal exponents are permitted, and only small ones.
        if not isinstance(right, ast.Constant) or not isinstance(right.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("exponent must be a numeric literal")
        if abs(right.value) > MAX_EXPONENT:
            raise ExpressionError("exponent too large")


def _guard_result(value):
    if isinstance(value, complex):
        raise ExpressionError("complex results are not supported")
    if isinstance(value, float) and not math.isfinite(value):
        raise ExpressionError("result is not finite")
    if abs(value) > MAX_ABS_RESULT:
        raise ExpressionError("result out of range")
    return value


def _eval(node: ast.AST, names: dict[str, float]):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, names)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        return names[node.id]
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        return _guard_result(_UNARY_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand, names)))
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        left = _eval(node
完整执行轨迹

回答

# Securing Mathematical Operations in Python

Below is a practical, OWASP-aligned approach. I validated sample inputs against the expression grammar and generated a security-hardening assessment — here's how to put it together.

## 1. Validation Evidence (what the grammar catches)

| Input | Result | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `2+3*4` | ✅ Valid | Safe arithmetic |
| `(2+3)*4 - 10/2` | ✅ Valid → `15` | Evaluates correctly |
| `sqrt(x) + 2*y` | ✅ Valid (with `allow_variables=true`) | Variables permitted only when explicitly enabled |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ Rejected — *"Unexpected character '_'"* | Code-injection attempt blocked at the syntax layer |

**Key takeaway:** a strict grammar that rejects identifiers/underscores/attribute access is your first line of defense. Never pass raw user input to `eval()`/`exec()`.

## 2. The Core Rule: Never `eval()` Untrusted Input

`eval()`/`exec()`/`compile()` on user input is the #1 arbitrary-code-execution risk (OWASP **A03: Injection**, CWE-95). Even "sandboxed" `eval` with `{"__builtins__": None}` is bypassable. Use an **AST allow-list evaluator** instead.

## 3. Safe Evaluator (AST allow-list pattern)

```python
import ast
import operator as op

# Only these node types and operators are permitted
_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: op.add, ast.Sub: op.sub, ast.Mult: op.mul,
    ast.Div: op.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: op.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: op.mod, ast.Pow: op.pow,
}
_ALLOWED_UNARY = {ast.UAdd: op.pos, ast.USub: op.neg}
_ALLOWED_FUNCS = {"sqrt": __import__("math").sqrt, "abs": abs}

MAX_EXPR_LEN = 256          # DoS guard: input size
MAX_POW_EXP = 1000          # DoS guard: 9**9**9 style blowups
MAX_DEPTH = 20              # DoS guard: nesting depth

def safe_eval(expr: str, variables: dict | None = None) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_EXPR_LEN:
        raise ValueError("Expression too long or invalid type")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        raise ValueError(f"Invalid syntax: {e}") from None

    if _depth(tree) > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ValueError("Expression nesting too deep")

    variables = variables or {}

    def _eval(node):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(node.value, bool):
                return node.value
            raise ValueError("Only numeric constants allowed")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_BINOPS:
            left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow) and abs(right) > MAX_POW_EXP:
                raise ValueError("Exponent too large")
            return _ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_UNARY:
            return _ALLOWED_UNARY[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id in variables:
            return variables[node.id]
        if (isinstance(node, ast.Call) and isinstance(node.func, ast.Name)
                and node.func.id in _ALLOWED_FUNCS and not node.keywords):
            return _ALLOWED_FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a) for a in node.args])
        raise ValueError(f"Disallowed construct: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval(tree)

def _depth(node, level=1):
    children = list(ast.iter_child_nodes(node))
    return level if not children else max(_depth(c, level + 1) for c in children)
```

This blocks attribute access (`__class__`), imports, comprehensions, lambdas, and string/bytes constants — the usual escape hatches.

## 4. OWASP-Aligned Recommendations

**A03 – Injection (Critical)**
- Replace all `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on user input with the AST allow-list above.
- Reject identifiers, underscores, and attribute access at the grammar level (as the validator demonstrated).
- If you must support variables, resolve them from an explicit dict — never from `globals()`/`locals()`.

**A04 – Insecure Design**
- Apply **defense in depth**: grammar validation → AST allow-list → resource limits → output type check.
- Fail closed: any unrecognized node type raises, never silently passes.

**A05 – Security Misconfiguration / DoS (A04/A06)**
- Enforce `MAX_EXPR_LEN`, `MAX_DEPTH`, and exponent caps to prevent CPU/memory exhaustion (`9**9**9`, deeply nested parens).
- Run evaluation in a subprocess with a timeout and memory limit for untrusted/high-volume input.

**A08 – Software & Data Integrity**
- Avoid `pickle`/`marshal` for expression payloads; use JSON.
- Pin and audit dependencies (`sympy`, `numexpr`) — see A06 below.

**A06 – Vulnerable & Outdated Components**
- Run `pip-audit` / `safety` in CI; pin versions and monitor CVEs for any math/parsing library.

**A09 – Logging & Monitoring**
- Log rejected expressions (with the offending construct) at WARNING; alert on spikes — they signal probing.
- Never log full raw input if it may contain secrets; truncate and sanitize.

**A02 – Cryptographic Failures**
- If expressions or results cross a network, enforce TLS; don't roll your own crypto for any signing of results.

## 5. Security Test Cases

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "9**9**9",                       # DoS
    "(" * 500 + "1" + ")" * 500,     # depth DoS
    "x" * 10_000,                    # length DoS
    "lambda: 1",
    "[i for i in range(10)]",
])
def test_rejects_dangerous(bad):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        safe_eval(bad)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("2+3*4", 14), ("(2+3)*4 - 10/2", 15), ("2**10", 1024),
])
def test_allows_arithmetic(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected

def test_variables_opt_in():
    assert safe_eval("x + 1", {"x": 41}) == 42
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        safe_eval("y + 1")            # undefined variable
```

## 6. Quick Checklist

- [ ] No `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on user input anywhere
- [ ] AST allow-list with explicit operator/function whitelist
- [ ] Length, depth, and exponent limits enforced
- [ ] Variables resolved from an explicit dict only
- [ ] Subprocess + timeout for untrusted/high-volume evaluation
- [ ] Rejections logged and monitored
- [ ] Dependencies pinned and audited (`pip-audit`)
- [ ] Security tests cover injection, DoS, and valid-arithmetic cases

**Bottom line:** validate with a strict grammar first (as shown — it already rejects `__import__(...)`), then evaluate through an AST allow-list with resource caps, and layer OWASP A03/A04/A06/A09 controls around it. This gives you safe math without ever handing user input to the Python interpreter.

*Note: these recommendations are general best practices — validate against your specific threat model and run a penetration test before production.*

Here's a complete, OWASP-aligned approach to securing mathematical operations in Python — grounded in the live validation results above.

## The core principle: never `eval()` untrusted input

The single biggest risk in "math in Python" is `eval()` / `exec()`. Even with `{"__builtins__": None}`, sandbox escapes are well-documented. The safe pattern is a **strict grammar / allowlist parser** that only understands arithmetic.

The validation runs above demonstrate exactly this:

| Expression | Result | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1` | ✅ valid → `9` | Legitimate arithmetic passes |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`Unexpected character '_'`) | **Code injection blocked** (OWASP A03) |
| `2 ** 10` / `9 ** 9 ** 9 ** 9` | ❌ rejected | Grammar doesn't even allow `**` — a classic **DoS vector** |
| `999…9 * 2` (50 digits) | ✅ → `2e+50` | Parser accepts huge literals → **you still need magnitude limits** |
| `1 / 0` | → `null` (graceful) | Division-by-zero handled, not a crash |

The lesson: a strict grammar kills injection and exponent-bomb DoS, but **you must add application-layer limits** for length, magnitude, and complexity.

## Reference implementation: allowlist AST evaluator

```python
import ast
import operator

_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub, ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod, ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

MAX_LEN, MAX_NODES, MAX_DEPTH = 200, 100, 20
MAX_INT_DIGITS, MAX_EXPONENT = 100, 1000

class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    pass

def safe_eval(expr: str):
    if not isinstance(expr, str):
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression must be a string")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too long")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"invalid syntax: {e.msg}") from None
    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too complex")
    return _eval_node(tree.body, depth=0)

def _eval_node(node, depth):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too deeply nested")
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        v = node.value
        if isinstance(v, bool) or not isinstance(v, (int, float)):
            raise UnsafeExpression("only numeric constants allowed")
        if isinstance(v, int) and len(str(abs(v))) > MAX_INT_DIGITS:
            raise UnsafeExpression("integer literal too large")
        return v
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = type(node.op)
        if op not in _ALLOWED_BINOPS:
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"operator not allowed: {op.__name__}")
        left, right = _eval_node(node.left, depth + 1), _eval_node(node.right, depth + 1)
        if op is ast.Pow and abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
            raise UnsafeExpression("exponent too large")
        if op in (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod) and right == 0:
            raise UnsafeExpression("division by zero")
        return _ALLOWED_BINOPS[op](left, right)
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = type(node.op)
        if op not in _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS:
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"unary operator not allowed: {op.__name__}")
        return _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS[op](_eval_node(node.operand, depth + 1))
    raise UnsafeExpression(f"disallowed element: {type(node).__name__}")
```

This rejects `Name`, `Call`, `Attribute`, `Subscript`, `Lambda`, comprehensions, and any operator not in the allowlist — closing the injection surface entirely.

## Defense-in-depth checklist

**Input validation (OWASP A03 Injection)**
- Allowlist characters/operators; reject anything else.
- Enforce max length, max AST depth, max node count.
- Cap integer digit count and exponent magnitude (blocks `9**9**9`-style bombs).
- Validate server-side always — never trust client-side checks.

**Resource limits (OWASP A04 Insecure Design)**
- For truly untrusted input, run evaluation in a **subprocess with `resource.setrlimit`** (`RLIMIT_CPU`, `RLIMIT_AS`) or a `signal.alarm` timeout. A pure-Python loop can't be interrupted mid-`**`, so a hard wall-clock limit is essential.
- Prefer `decimal.Decimal` / `fractions.Fraction` for money — never `float` for financial math.

**Error handling (OWASP A05 Misconfiguration)**
- Return generic errors to users; log details internally. Never leak stack traces or internals.

**Dependencies (OWASP A06 Vulnerable Components)**
- Prefer a vetted library over hand-rolling: **`simpleeval`** (small, allowlist-based) or **`asteval`** (has safety modes). Avoid `numexpr`/`sympy` for untrusted input — they're DoS-prone.
- Pin versions and run `pip-audit` / `safety` in CI.

**Data integrity (OWASP A08)**
- Never `pickle.loads()` numeric payloads from untrusted sources — use JSON.

**Logging (OWASP A09)**
- Log every rejected expression (with source/IP) for detection and monitoring.

## Security test cases to add

```python
import pytest
@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",   # code injection
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",       # file access
    "9**9**9**9",                       # exponent DoS
    "1/0",                              # divide by zero
    "(" * 500 + "1" + ")" * 500,        # nesting DoS
    "x + 1",                            # undefined name
])
def test_rejects_unsafe(bad):
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        safe_eval(bad)

def test_accepts_arithmetic():
    assert safe_eval("(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1") == 9
```

## Bottom line

1. **Never** `eval()`/`exec()` untrusted math — use an allowlist AST parser (or `simpleeval`).
2. **Layer limits** on top: length, depth, magnitude, exponent, and a hard timeout.
3. **Map to OWASP**: A03 (injection), A04 (design/DoS), A05 (error leakage), A06 (deps), A08 (deserialization), A09 (logging).
4. **Test adversarially** with the injection and DoS cases above.

The tool-generated OWASP assessment prompt (from the security-hardening builder) is available if you want to run a full codebase audit — it includes the complete A01–A10 checklist, risk-rating matrix, and remediation guidance tailored to Python. Want me to adapt that prompt to a specific module or framework (FastAPI, Django, Flask)?

## What the live validation showed

I ran four checks against a strict-grammar validator:

| Expression | Variables allowed | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `2 + 3 * (4 - 1) / 2` | no | ✅ valid → evaluates to `6.5` |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | no | ❌ **rejected at parse** — `Unexpected character '_'` |
| `x + y * 2` | yes | ✅ valid → `18` (with `x=10, y=4`) |

The RCE payload died at the *lexer*, before any evaluation — that is the whole point: a whitelist grammar fails closed. Note that `('` would also be rejected; the grammar has no string literal, no attribute access, and no call syntax.

One caveat on the run: `batch_evaluate` returned `Error: this.toolHandlers.handleBatchEvaluate is not a function` — a harness defect, not a result. Ignore it; use single `evaluate` calls.

**Do not ship the MCP tool itself as your production evaluator.** It's a sandbox, not a hardened library. Build the equivalent in-process with the layers below.

---

## The one rule that matters

> `eval()`, `exec()`, and `compile()` on untrusted input are **code injection**, not input validation. There is no "sanitize then eval" that is safe.

Instead: **parse → whitelist the AST → interpret the tree yourself.** You never hand user text to the Python compiler at runtime, so the attacker's grammar is your grammar.

---

## Reference implementation

```python
from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import decimal
import logging
import math

logger = logging.getLogger("mathsafe")

class ExpressionRejected(ValueError):
    """Validation or domain failure on a user-supplied expression."""

MAX_LENGTH     = 512
MAX_DEPTH      = 20
MAX_NODES      = 200
MAX_ABS_OPERAND = decimal.Decimal("1e15")
MAX_EXPONENT   = 64

_ALLOWED_FUNCS = {"abs", "min", "max", "round"}
_BIN_OPS = {ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod, ast.Pow}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd, ast.USub}

CTX = decimal.Context(
    prec=28,
    traps=[decimal.InvalidOperation, decimal.DivisionByZero, decimal.Overflow],
)


# ---------- 1. Parse ----------
def parse(expr: str) -> ast.Expression:
    if not isinstance(expr, str):
        raise ExpressionRejected("expression must be a string")
    if not expr.strip():
        raise ExpressionRejected("expression is empty")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LENGTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected(f"expression exceeds {MAX_LENGTH} characters")
    try:
        return ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        # Report position only; never echo the payload back into logs verbatim.
        raise ExpressionRejected(f"syntax error at column {exc.offset}") from exc


# ---------- 2. Whitelist the AST ----------
def assert_safe(node, env, depth=0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected("nesting depth exceeded")

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        _check_value(node.value)
        return

    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if not isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Load):
            raise ExpressionRejected("assignment not permitted")
        if node.id not in env:                       # explicit allow-list only
            raise ExpressionRejected(f"unknown identifier: {node.id}")
        _check_value(env[node.id])
        return

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _BIN_OPS:
            raise ExpressionRejected(f"operator not permitted: {type(node.op).__name__}")
        _check_pow(node)
        assert_safe(node.left, env, depth + 1)
        assert_safe(node.right, env, depth + 1)
        return

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _UNARY_OPS:
            raise ExpressionRejected("unary operator not permitted")
        assert_safe(node.operand, env, depth + 1)
        return

    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _ALLOWED_FUNCS:
            raise ExpressionRejected("function call not permitted")
        if node.keywords or not (1 <= len(node.args) <= 2):
            raise ExpressionRejected("unsupported call signature")
        for arg in node.args:
            assert_safe(arg, env, depth + 1)
        return

    # Everything else — Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehension, JoinedStr,
    # Starred, Await, walrus, await, etc. — is denied by default.
    raise ExpressionRejected(f"node type not permitted: {type(node).__name__}")


def _check_value(value):
    # bool is an int subclass -> must be rejected explicitly
    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, (int, float, decimal.Decimal)):
        raise ExpressionRejected(f"unsupported literal type: {type(value).__name__}")
    if isinstance(value, float) and not math.isfinite(value):
        raise ExpressionRejected("non-finite literal")
    if abs(value) > MAX_ABS_OPERAND:
        raise ExpressionRejected("operand out of permitted range")


def _check_pow(node):
    if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
        r = node.right
        if not (isinstance(r, ast.Constant) and isinstance(r.value, int) and not isinstance(r.value, bool)):
            raise ExpressionRejected("exponent must be an integer literal")
        if abs(r.value) > MAX_EXPONENT:
            raise ExpressionRejected("exponent exceeds safe limit")


def _node_count(tree):
    return sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree))


# ---------- 3. Interpret the tree (never compile it) ----------
def _to_decimal(v) -> decimal.Decimal:
    return v if isinstance(v, decimal.Decimal) else decimal.Decimal(str(v))


def _eval(node, env):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return _to_decimal(node.value)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        return _to_decimal(env[node.id])
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        operand = _eval(node.operand, env)
        return operand if isinstance(node.op, ast.UAdd) else CTX.minus(operand)
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        left, right = _eval(node.left, env), _eval(node.right, env)
        op = type(node.op)
        if op is ast.Add:      return CTX.add(left, right)
        if op is ast.Sub:      return CTX.subtract(left, right)
        if op is ast.Mult:     return CTX.multiply(left, right)
        if op is ast.Div:      return CTX.divide(left, right)
        if op is ast.FloorDiv: return CTX.divide_int(left, right)
        if op is ast.Mod:      return CTX.remainder(left, right)
        if op is ast.Pow:
            return CTX.power(left, int(right))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        args = [_eval(a, env) for a in node.args]
        fn = node.func.id
        if fn == "abs":   return CTX.abs(args[0])
        if fn == "min":   return CTX.min(*args)
        if fn == "max":   return CTX.max(*args)
        if fn == "round":
            return args[0].quantize(decimal.Decimal(1).scaleb(-int(args[1]))) if len(args) == 2 else args[0].to_in
完整执行轨迹

## What the validation probes actually showed

I ran your two concerns — expression validation and OWASP-aligned review — against live tooling. Results:

| Expression | Verdict | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * 2 / (c - 1)` | ✅ valid | Normal arithmetic parses cleanly |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected — `Unexpected character '_'` | A strict grammar kills the classic `eval()` RCE payload at the lexer, before any tree walk |
| `a.b + c[0]` | ❌ rejected | Attribute access and subscripts — the backbone of sandbox-escape chains — never parse |
| `lambda x: x + 1` | ❌ rejected | No callables, no closures |
| `((((((((((1+2))))))))))` | ✅ **accepted** | 10 levels of nesting passed. Grammar validation alone does **not** bound complexity — you need explicit depth/node caps |
| `sqrt(16) + 2^3 - 1` | → `11` | ⚠️ Here `^` means **exponent**. In Python `^` is **XOR**. Silent semantic mismatch |
| `1/0` | → `result: null`, `isError: false` | ⚠️ **Silent failure.** Division by zero returned null instead of raising. Never replicate this |

Two findings worth flagging before anything else: the `1/0` case returned a null result with no error signal, and `^` was interpreted as exponentiation rather than Python's XOR. Both are the kind of quiet wrongness that becomes a data-integrity bug rather than a crash.

---

## The core rule: `eval()` is not a math function

`eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})` is **not** a sandbox. The builtins restriction is bypassed in one line:

```python
().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()   # reach subprocess.Popen, os, etc.
```

Any design where untrusted text reaches `eval`, `exec`, or `compile` is CWE-95 (Improper Neutralization of Directives in Dynamically Evaluated Code) and maps to **OWASP A03:2021 – Injection**. The fix is not filtering — it is *parsing into a data structure and interpreting only an allowlisted subset of it*.

---

## A safe evaluator

```python
import ast
import math
import operator

MAX_LENGTH   = 256      # characters
MAX_DEPTH    = 20       # AST nesting
MAX_NODES    = 100      # total AST nodes
MAX_EXPONENT = 64       # blocks 9**9**9 CPU bombs
MAX_ABS      = 1e12     # result magnitude ceiling

_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,       ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,      ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}


class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Message is deliberately generic — safe to return to the client."""


def _validate(node, depth, counter):
    counter[0] += 1
    if counter[0] > MAX_NODES:
        raise ExpressionError("Expression is too complex.")
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionError("Expression is too deeply nested.")

    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _validate(node.body, depth + 1, counter)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — reject it explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("Only numeric literals are allowed.")
        return

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _BIN_OPS:
            raise ExpressionError("Operator not allowed.")
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if not (isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant)
                    and isinstance(node.right.value, (int, float))
                    and abs(node.right.value) <= MAX_EXPONENT):
                raise ExpressionError("Exponent must be a small numeric literal.")
        _validate(node.left, depth + 1, counter)
        _validate(node.right, depth + 1, counter)
        return

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _UNARY_OPS:
            raise ExpressionError("Operator not allowed.")
        _validate(node.operand, depth + 1, counter)
        return

    # Attribute, Subscript, Call, Lambda, Comprehension, JoinedStr, ...
    raise ExpressionError("Unsupported expression element.")


def _eval(node):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            raise ExpressionError("Division by zero.")
        try:
            return _BIN_OPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
        except (OverflowError, ZeroDivisionError, ValueError):
            raise ExpressionError("Arithmetic error.") from None
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        return _UNARY_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
    raise ExpressionError("Unsupported expression element.")


def safe_eval(expression: str) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise ExpressionError("Expression must be a string.")
    if len(expression) > MAX_LENGTH:
        raise ExpressionError("Expression is too long.")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, RecursionError, MemoryError):
        raise ExpressionError("Expression is not valid syntax.") from None

    _validate(tree, 0, [0])
    result = _eval(tree)

    if isinstance(result, complex):
        raise ExpressionError("Complex results are not supported.")
    if not math.isfinite(result):
        raise ExpressionError("Result is not a finite number.")
    if abs(result) > MAX_ABS:
        raise ExpressionError("Result is out of the permitted range.")
    return float(result)
```

Design notes that matter:

- **`mode="eval"`** — rejects statements, assignments, and imports at the parser level.
- **Allowlist, not denylist** — the final `raise` catches every node type you didn't explicitly permit, including future Python AST additions.
- **`bool` rejected explicitly** — `True + True` would otherwise silently equal `2`.
- **Exponent capped** — `9**9**9` is a one-character CPU exhaustion attack (CWE-400).
- **`math.isfinite`** — blocks `inf`/`nan` propagation into downstream storage.
- **Generic messages** — no stack traces, no AST dumps, no internal paths.

For money, swap `float` for `Decimal` and reject `ast.Pow` entirely; binary floats cannot represent `0.1` exactly and rounding drift in financial totals is a correctness defect, not just a style preference.

---

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| OWASP | Risk in this context | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval()` on user input → RCE (CWE-95) | AST allowlist; never `eval`/`exec`/`compile` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No length/depth/node caps → CPU & memory DoS (CWE-400) | `MAX_LENGTH`, `MAX_DEPTH`, `MAX_NODES`, `MAX_EXPONENT` |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Tracebacks and AST dumps leaked to clients | Generic `ExpressionError`; details only in server logs |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | Unpinned `simpleeval`/`sympy` supply chain | Pin + hash-lock dependencies; audit CVEs |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Rejected payloads invisible → no attack detection | Log rejections with correlation ID, source IP, hash of input |
| **A01 / A07** | Unauthenticated endpoint abused as a compute oracle | Auth + per-user rate limiting on the expression endpoint |

---

## Test cases to lock the behavior in

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("1 + 2 * 3", 7.0),
    ("(1 + 2) * 3", 9.0),
    ("-4 + 10", 6.0),
    ("7 % 3", 1.0),
])
def test_valid(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",   # RCE
    "().__class__.__bases__[0]",       # sandbox escape
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",      # file read
    "lambda x: x",                     # callable
    "[x for x in range(10)]",          # comprehension
    "a.b",                             # attribute
    "c[0]",                            # subscript
    "1/0",                             # division by zero
    "9**9**9",                         # CPU bomb
    "1" * 500,                         # length bomb
    "(" * 50 + "1" + ")" * 50,         # depth bomb
    "float('nan')",                    # non-finite
    "True + True",                     # bool coercion
])
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(ExpressionError):
        safe_eval(expr)
```

## Defense in depth

1. **Process isolation** — run the evaluator in a subprocess with `resource.setrlimit(RLIMIT_CPU, ...)` and `RLIMIT_AS`. The AST walk is safe by construction, but a resource ceiling means a parser bug is a 500, not an outage.
2. **Rate limit** — even a correct evaluator is a free compute service. Cap requests per user per minute.
3. **Structured logging** — log `{correlation_id, user_id, input_hash, rejection_reason}`. Never log the raw payload if it may contain secrets.
4. **Prefer a vetted library** where the grammar is fixed: `simpleeval` (with a restricted function set) or `sympy.sympify` with `

回答

## What I verified first

I ran the expressions through the validator/evaluator before writing recommendations. The results are the most useful part of the answer:

| Expression | Validator verdict | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1` | ✅ valid → evaluates to `9` | Legitimate arithmetic works |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | Grammar has no `_` / call-chain support |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | No attribute access |
| `1; import os` | ❌ rejected | Statement separator blocked |
| `2**999999999` | ❌ rejected | **But only because `**` isn't in the grammar** — not because of a DoS guard |
| `((((…20 levels…))))` with `max_depth=10` | ⚠️ **"syntactically valid"** | **`max_depth` was not enforced** |
| `x + 1` with `allow_variables=false` | ⚠️ **"syntactically valid"** | **`allow_variables` was not enforced** |
| `eval(1)` | ⚠️ **"syntactically valid"** | Arbitrary function *names* pass the grammar |
| `1/0` | ⚠️ evaluates to `null` | Division by zero silently swallowed |
| `1e308 * 10` | ⚠️ evaluates to `null` | Overflow to `inf` silently swallowed |

**The core lesson: syntax validation is not security validation.** A grammar check tells you the string is well-formed arithmetic. It does not tell you the *names* are safe, the *cost* is bounded, or the *result* is finite. Two of the flags I passed (`allow_variables`, `max_depth`) were accepted but had no effect — so never treat a validator's "valid" as an authorization decision.

---

## 1. Never use `eval()` — use an AST allowlist

`eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})` is **not** a sandbox. It is bypassable via `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()`, and it cannot be fixed by adding more names to the blocklist. Use `ast.parse(..., mode="eval")` and walk the tree yourself, rejecting every node type you did not explicitly allow.

```python
# safe_math.py
from __future__ import annotations
import ast, math, operator

class ExpressionRejected(ValueError):
    """Internal reason. Never return this text to the client."""

MAX_LENGTH   = 256
MAX_DEPTH    = 20
MAX_NODES    = 100
MAX_EXPONENT = 64
MAX_ABS      = 1e100

_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,       ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,      ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

# name -> (callable, min_args, max_args)
_FUNCS = {
    "abs": (abs, 1, 1),        "min": (min, 1, 8),
    "max": (max, 1, 8),        "round": (round, 1, 2),
    "sqrt": (math.sqrt, 1, 1), "log": (math.log, 1, 2),
    "exp": (math.exp, 1, 1),   "floor": (math.floor, 1, 1),
    "ceil": (math.ceil, 1, 1),
}

def _guard(value):
    if isinstance(value, complex):
        raise ExpressionRejected("complex result")
    if isinstance(value, float) and not math.isfinite(value):
        raise ExpressionRejected("non-finite result")
    if abs(value) > MAX_ABS:
        raise ExpressionRejected("result magnitude exceeded")
    return value

def _max_depth(node: ast.AST) -> int:
    """Iterative — a recursive depth check is itself a DoS vector."""
    stack, best = [(node, 1)], 0
    while stack:
        n, d = stack.pop()
        best = max(best, d)
        if best > MAX_DEPTH:
            return best
        stack.extend((c, d + 1) for c in ast.iter_child_nodes(n))
    return best

def _eval(node: ast.AST, depth: int = 0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected("depth exceeded")

    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, depth + 1)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — reject it explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionRejected("non-numeric literal")
        return node.value

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = _UNARYOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ExpressionRejected("unary operator not allowed")
        return _guard(op(_eval(node.operand, depth + 1)))

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BINOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ExpressionRejected("binary operator not allowed")
        left  = _eval(node.left,  depth + 1)
        right = _eval(node.right, depth + 1)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow) and abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
            raise ExpressionRejected("exponent too large")
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            raise ExpressionRejected("division by zero")
        return _guard(op(left, right))

    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):
            raise ExpressionRejected("indirect call")
        entry = _FUNCS.get(node.func.id)
        if entry is None:
            raise ExpressionRejected("function not allowed")
        fn, lo, hi = entry
        if node.keywords or not (lo <= len(node.args) <= hi):
            raise ExpressionRejected("bad call signature")
        return _guard(fn(*(_eval(a, depth + 1) for a in node.args)))

    # Attribute, Subscript, Name, Lambda, Comprehension, Starred, ... all land here
    raise ExpressionRejected(f"node type not allowed: {type(node).__name__}")

def safe_eval(expression: str):
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise ExpressionRejected("not a string")
    if len(expression) > MAX_LENGTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected("too long")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")   # mode="eval" rejects statements
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, MemoryError, RecursionError) as exc:
        raise ExpressionRejected("malformed") from exc
    if _max_depth(tree) > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected("depth exceeded")
    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        raise ExpressionRejected("node count exceeded")
    return _eval(tree)
```

Why each guard exists, tied to the evidence above:

- `mode="eval"` — kills `1; import os` at the parser, before your code runs.
- `ast.Name` not in the allowlist — kills `x + 1` regardless of any `allow_variables` flag.
- `_FUNCS` lookup by exact string — kills `eval(1)` and `__import__(...)`, which the grammar happily accepted.
- `MAX_EXPONENT` — the real fix for `2
完整执行轨迹

回答

# Securing Mathematical Operations in Python

Here's a complete, practical guide based on the OWASP-aligned assessment and the live validation demonstrations above.

## The Core Threat: `eval()` / `exec()`

The single biggest risk in "math evaluation" code is using Python's built-in `eval()` or `exec()` on user input. As the demonstrations above show, a strict grammar validator **rejects** dangerous payloads before they ever run:

| Expression | Result |
|---|---|
| `2 + 3 * (4 - 1) / 2` | ✅ Valid → evaluates to `6.5` |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ Rejected — `Unexpected character '_'` |
| `(lambda: 1)()` | ❌ Rejected — lambda/call syntax not allowed |

This is the essence of **allowlist-based validation**: only permit the exact grammar you need (numbers, `+ - * / ** %`, parentheses, whitelisted functions), and reject everything else.

## Recommended Architecture: AST Whitelisting

Never `eval()` raw input. Instead, parse with `ast` and walk the tree, permitting only safe node types:

```python
import ast
import operator

# 1. Allowlist of permitted operators
_ALLOWED_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.USub: operator.neg,
    ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
}

# 2. Allowlist of permitted functions (keep minimal!)
_ALLOWED_FUNCS = {
    "abs": abs,
    "round": round,
    "min": min,
    "max": max,
}

class SafeEvaluator(ast.NodeVisitor):
    def visit_Expression(self, node):
        return self.visit(node.body)

    def visit_Constant(self, node):
        # Only allow numeric literals — reject strings, bytes, etc.
        if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(node.value, bool):
            return node.value
        raise ValueError(f"Disallowed constant: {node.value!r}")

    def visit_BinOp(self, node):
        op = _ALLOWED_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ValueError(f"Disallowed operator: {type(node.op).__name__}")
        return op(self.visit(node.left), self.visit(node.right))

    def visit_UnaryOp(self, node):
        op = _ALLOWED_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ValueError(f"Disallowed unary operator: {type(node.op).__name__}")
        return op(self.visit(node.operand))

    def visit_Call(self, node):
        # Only allow calls to whitelisted plain-name functions
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _ALLOWED_FUNCS:
            raise ValueError("Disallowed function call")
        if node.keywords:
            raise ValueError("Keyword arguments not allowed")
        args = [self.visit(a) for a in node.args]
        return _ALLOWED_FUNCS[node.func.id](*args)

    def generic_visit(self, node):
        # Reject ANY node type not explicitly handled above
        raise ValueError(f"Disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")


def safe_eval(expression: str, *, max_length: int = 1000) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise TypeError("Expression must be a string")
    if len(expression) > max_length:
        raise ValueError("Expression too long")          # DoS guard
    tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")            # Parse only, never exec
    return SafeEvaluator().visit(tree)
```

**Why this is safe:** `generic_visit` raises on *anything* not explicitly whitelisted — so `__import__`, attribute access (`x.y`), subscripts, comprehensions, lambdas, and f-strings are all rejected by default (fail-closed).

## OWASP Top 10 Mapping

| OWASP Category | How it applies here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03: Injection** | `eval`/`exec` = arbitrary code execution | AST allowlist; never `eval` user input |
| **A04: Insecure Design** | No threat model for expression input | Fail-closed design, minimal grammar |
| **A05: Security Misconfiguration** | Overly permissive builtins | Restrict `__builtins__`, disable `eval` |
| **A08: Data/Integrity Failures** | Untrusted input deserialization | Validate before processing |
| **A09: Logging Failures** | Silent failures hide attacks | Log rejected expressions |

## Additional Hardening Recommendations

1. **Denial-of-Service guards** — cap expression length, nesting depth, and exponent size (`9**9**9**9` can hang a CPU). Add a recursion/depth limit and reject huge `Pow` operands.
2. **Never expose `__builtins__`** — if you must use `eval` (avoid it), pass `{"__builtins__": {}}` as globals. But prefer the AST approach.
3. **Type discipline** — reject `bool` (subclass of `int`), strings, and complex numbers unless explicitly needed.
4. **Error handling** — catch `SyntaxError`, `ValueError`, `TypeError`, `ZeroDivisionError`, `OverflowError` and return a generic error to the caller (don't leak internals).
5. **Logging & monitoring (A09)** — log every rejected expression with source IP/user for intrusion detection.
6. **Dependency security (A06)** — if using a library like `simpleeval`, `asteval`, or `numexpr`, pin versions and scan for CVEs (`pip-audit`, `safety`).
7. **Resource limits** — run evaluation in a subprocess with CPU/memory limits (`resource.setrlimit`) for untrusted multi-tenant scenarios.
8. **Test cases** — include adversarial inputs: `__import__('os')`, `open('/etc/passwd')`, `(lambda:1)()`, `[x for x in ()]`, `1e999999`, deeply nested parens.

## Using the Validation Tools Directly

The MCP tools demonstrated above give you a **first line of defense** — a strict grammar validator that rejects non-arithmetic syntax before evaluation:

- **`validate_syntax`** — checks an expression against a strict math grammar (returns syntax errors for anything non-mathematical).
- **`evaluate`** — validates *and* computes, refusing to run invalid/malicious input.
- **`set_variable` / `get_variable`** — store results in a sandboxed math context.

This "validate-then-evaluate" pattern is exactly what you should replicate in your Python code: **parse → whitelist-check → compute**, never `eval` directly.

## Summary Checklist

- [ ] Replace all `eval()`/`exec()` on user input with AST allowlist evaluation
- [ ] Whitelist only needed operators and functions; fail closed on everything else
- [ ] Add length, depth, and exponent DoS limits
- [ ] Reject non-numeric constants and `bool`
- [ ] Handle and log all exceptions; don't leak internals
- [ ] Pin and audit math-parsing dependencies
- [ ] Add adversarial test cases to your suite
- [ ] Consider subprocess isolation for untrusted multi-tenant use

The live demo confirms the principle: legitimate arithmetic (`6.5`) passes cleanly, while code-injection and lambda payloads are rejected at the syntax layer — before any execution occurs.

## What the validation probes showed

I ran your expressions through the strict-grammar validator and evaluator. Results are instructive:

| Input | Syntax check | Evaluation | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| `2 * (3 + 4) / 7 - 1` | ✅ valid | `1` | Normal arithmetic is fine |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`_` unexpected) | — | Grammar blocks dunder/attribute access |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | — | No string literals / calls to arbitrary names |
| `9 ** 9 ** 9` | ❌ rejected | — | `**` not in grammar → no exponent-bomb |
| `((((((((((1+1))))))))))` | ✅ valid | — | **Depth is not limited** → stack/DoS risk |
| `x + 1` | ✅ valid | — | **Identifiers pass syntax** → must be rejected at eval |
| `1/0` | ✅ valid | `null` | Division-by-zero must be caught, not crash |
| `sqrt(16) + abs(-3)` | ✅ valid | `7` | Only whitelisted functions should resolve |

The takeaway: **syntax validation is necessary but not sufficient.** `x + 1` and deep nesting both pass syntax yet are unsafe to hand to `eval()`. You need a *semantic* whitelist on top of syntax checking.

---

## The core rule: never `eval()` user math

`eval()`/`exec()` on user input is **OWASP A03: Injection** (and CWE-95, code injection). Even "sanitized" input can reach `__builtins__`, `__class__`, `__subclasses__`, etc. The safe pattern is **parse → walk the AST → allow only whitelisted node types → evaluate yourself**.

### Secure evaluator (drop-in reference)

```python
import ast
import operator
from math import sqrt, abs as _abs  # only what you expose

# 1. Whitelist of allowed AST node types
_ALLOWED_NODES = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Constant,
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.Mod, ast.Pow,
    ast.USub, ast.UAdd, ast.Load,
)

# 2. Whitelist of operators -> callables
_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod, ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.USub: operator.neg, ast.UAdd: operator.pos}

# 3. Whitelist of functions (NO builtins, NO attribute access)
_FUNCS = {"sqrt": sqrt, "abs": _abs}

# 4. Hard limits (defense in depth)
MAX_LEN = 200          # characters
MAX_DEPTH = 20         # AST nesting
MAX_NODES = 100        # total AST nodes
MAX_EXPONENT = 100     # blocks 9**9**9 style bombs
MAX_ABS_VALUE = 1e12   # result magnitude cap


class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    pass


def _check_limits(tree: ast.AST) -> None:
    depth = nodes = 0
    stack = [(tree, 1)]
    while stack:
        node, d = stack.pop()
        nodes += 1
        depth = max(depth, d)
        if nodes > MAX_NODES or depth > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise UnsafeExpression("expression too complex")
        stack.extend((child, d + 1) for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node))


def _eval(node: ast.AST):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        if not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) or isinstance(node.value, bool):
            raise UnsafeExpression("only numeric literals allowed")
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BIN_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"operator not allowed: {type(node.op).__name__}")
        left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow) and abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
            raise UnsafeExpression("exponent too large")
        result = op(left, right)
        if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_VALUE:
            raise UnsafeExpression("result out of range")
        return result
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = _UNARY_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise UnsafeExpression("unary operator not allowed")
        return op(_eval(node.operand))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _FUNCS:
            raise UnsafeExpression("function not allowed")
        if node.keywords:
            raise UnsafeExpression("keyword args not allowed")
        return _FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a) for a in node.args])
    raise UnsafeExpression(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")


def safe_eval(expression: str) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expression, str) or len(expression) > MAX_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too long")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise UnsafeExpression("invalid syntax") from exc

    # Reject any node type not explicitly whitelisted
    for n in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(n, _ALLOWED_NODES + (ast.Call, ast.Name)):
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"disallowed node: {type(n).__name__}")

    _check_limits(tree)
    return _eval(tree)
```

Why this is safe where `eval()` is not:
- **No `Name` resolution** except whitelisted functions → `x + 1` raises `UnsafeExpression`.
- **No `Attribute`/`Subscript`/`Lambda`/`Comprehension`** nodes → no `__class__` escape.
- **No string literals** → no `open(...)`.
- **Explicit `Pow` cap + result cap** → no `9**9**9` DoS.
- **Depth/node caps** → no stack exhaustion from `((((...))))`.

### Handling errors safely (OWASP A05 / A09)

```python
import logging
logger = logging.getLogger("math.api")

def handle(expr: str):
    try:
        return {"ok": True, "result": safe_eval(expr)}
    except UnsafeExpression as exc:
        # Log the *reason* server-side; return a generic message to the client
        logger.warning("rejected expression", extra={"reason": str(exc), "len": len(expr)})
        return {"ok": False, "error": "invalid expression"}, 400
    except ZeroDivisionError:
        return {"ok": False, "error": "division by zero"}, 400
    except Exception:
        logger.exception("unexpected eval failure")
        return {"ok": False, "error": "internal error"}, 500
```

Never echo the raw exception/stack trace to the user — that's information disclosure.

| OWASP | Risk here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval()` → RCE | AST whitelist evaluator above |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No resource limits | Length/depth/node/exponent caps |
| **A05 Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks, permissive CORS | Generic errors, strict CORS |
| **A09 Logging/Monitoring** | Blind to abuse | Log rejections + rate-limit hits |
| **A01 Access Control** | Open calculator abused | Auth + per-user rate limiting |

Add **rate limiting** (e.g., `slowapi`/`Flask-Limiter`) and a **request timeout** — a whitelisted evaluator still costs CPU, so cap requests/sec per client.
