回答

## Validation result (live check)

I ran the expression `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1` through the syntax validator and evaluator:

- **Syntax:** valid (balanced, depth 1, no variables)
- **Result:** `9` (type: number)

That's the "happy path." The security work is in making sure *only* that class of input is ever accepted.

---

## Threat model for math evaluation in Python

| Risk | OWASP | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| `eval()` / `exec()` on user input | **A03: Injection** | Arbitrary code execution: `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` |
| Attribute/name access in expressions | A03 / A01 | Sandbox escape via `().__class__.__bases__` |
| Unbounded `**` or huge literals | **A04: Insecure Design** | CPU/memory DoS: `9**9**9` |
| Deeply nested / very long input | A04 | Parser recursion, memory exhaustion |
| Verbose tracebacks returned to caller | **A05: Security Misconfiguration** | Leaks internals, aids attackers |
| No audit trail of rejected input | **A09: Logging Failures** | No detection of probing |

**Rule of thumb: never use `eval`, `exec`, or `compile` on untrusted input — not even with an "empty `__builtins__`."** That sandbox has been escaped repeatedly.

---

## Recommended pattern: AST whitelist evaluator

Parse, walk the tree, and reject anything that isn't a number or an allowed operator. This is the approach used by libraries like `asteval`/`simpleeval`.

```python
import ast
import operator
from decimal import Decimal, InvalidOperation

class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    """Raised when an expression violates the allowed grammar."""

# Whitelist: only these node types may appear in the tree.
_ALLOWED_NODES = (
    ast.Expression, ast.BinOp, ast.UnaryOp, ast.Constant,
    ast.Add, ast.Sub, ast.Mult, ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod,
    ast.Pow, ast.USub, ast.UAdd,
)

_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.USub: operator.neg, ast.UAdd: operator.pos}

MAX_LEN = 256          # characters
MAX_DEPTH = 20         # nesting levels
MAX_EXPONENT = 1000    # guard against 9**9**9
MAX_ABS_VALUE = Decimal("1e12")


def _check_limits(expr: str) -> None:
    if not isinstance(expr, str):
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression must be a string")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too long")
    if expr.count("(") + expr.count("[") > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too deeply nested")


def _eval_node(node: ast.AST) -> Decimal:
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval_node(node.body)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — reject it explicitly.
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise UnsafeExpression("only numeric literals are allowed")
        return Decimal(str(node.value))

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BIN_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"operator not allowed: {type(node.op).__name__}")
        left, right = _eval_node(node.left), _eval_node(node.right)

        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            if abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                raise UnsafeExpression("exponent out of range")
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            raise UnsafeExpression("division by zero")

        try:
            result = Decimal(op(left, right))
        except (InvalidOperation, OverflowError, ZeroDivisionError) as exc:
            raise UnsafeExpression("arithmetic error") from exc

        if abs(result) > MAX_ABS_VALUE:
            raise UnsafeExpression("result out of range")
        return result

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = _UNARY_OPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise UnsafeExpression("unary operator not allowed")
        return Decimal(op(_eval_node(node.operand)))

    # Anything else — Call, Name, Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehension,
    # f-string, walrus, etc. — is rejected here.
    raise UnsafeExpression(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")


def safe_eval(expr: str) -> Decimal:
    _check_limits(expr)
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise UnsafeExpression("malformed expression") from exc

    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(node, _ALLOWED_NODES):
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")

    return _eval_node(tree)
```

**Why this is safe:** the whitelist is *positive* (allow-list), not a blacklist of "bad" names. `Call`, `Name`, `Attribute`, `Subscript`, and `Lambda` simply have no code path, so `__import__`, `().__class__`, and `open` are unreachable by construction.

---

## Defense-in-depth checklist

1. **Validate before you evaluate.** Run a syntax/grammar check first (as the validator tool did above) and reject on the first failure — fail closed, never fall back to `eval`.
2. **Bound the input.** Length cap, nesting-depth cap, exponent cap, and result-magnitude cap. This is your DoS control (A04).
3. **Use `Decimal` for money/finance**, not `float` — avoids precision and rounding-integrity issues.
4. **Never echo raw exceptions.** Return a generic `"invalid expression"` to the caller; log the detail server-side (A05 + A09).
5. **Log rejections with context** (user id, input hash, reason) so probing is detectable. Don't log the full raw payload if it may contain secrets.
6. **Run untrusted evaluation out-of-process** if you must support heavier math — a subprocess or worker with a CPU/memory rlimit and a wall-clock timeout. In-process limits alone don't stop a determined DoS.
7. **Keep dependencies patched** (A06) — if you use `simpleeval`, `asteval`, or `sympy`, pin and audit them; `sympy.sympify` on untrusted input is *not* safe by default.
8. **Add a security regression suite** (below) and run it in CI.

---

## Tests worth having

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1", Decimal("9")),
    ("-5 + 2", Decimal("-3")),
    ("2 ** 10", Decimal("1024")),
])
def test_valid(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",   # code execution
    "().__class__.__bases__",          # sandbox escape
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",      # file access
    "x + 1",                           # names not allowed
    "9 ** 9 ** 9",                     # DoS
    "1 / 0",                           # arithmetic error
    "[" * 500,                         # parser exhaustion
    "True + 1",                        # bool masquerading as int
])
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        safe_eval(expr)
```

---

## Bottom line

Secure math evaluation is **grammar restriction, not sanitization**. Parse to an AST, allow only numeric literals and a fixed operator set, enforce size/exponent/result bounds, use `Decimal`, and fail closed with generic errors plus server-side logging. That covers OWASP **A03 (Injection)**, **A04 (Insecure Design)**, **A05 (Misconfiguration)**, and **A09 (Logging)** in one pass — and the validator confirmed the legitimate path still works (`(2+3)*4/2-1 → 9`).

## Short answer

Treat expression evaluation as a **two-phase pipeline: validate → evaluate**, and never let untrusted text reach `eval()`/`exec()`. I verified this pattern against the strict-grammar validator available here:

| Input | Result |
|---|---|
| `2+3*4` | ✅ syntactically valid |
| `(1+2)*3/4` | ✅ valid → evaluates to `2.25` |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected — `Unexpected character '_' at line 1, column 1` |

That third row is the whole point: a grammar that only admits numbers, operators, and parentheses makes code execution **structurally impossible**, not merely "filtered". Note also that `(1+2)**3/4` was rejected — `**` is not in that grammar. If you need exponentiation, add it to the allowlist deliberately (with a magnitude cap); do **not** fall back to `eval()` to get it.

---

## 1. The validator (allowlist, not blocklist)

```python
import ast, operator

MAX_LEN = 256
MAX_DEPTH = 20
MAX_EXPONENT = 64

_BIN_OPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,          # only if you truly need it
}
_UNARY_OPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}

class ExpressionRejected(ValueError):
    """Raised for any input outside the allowlist. Message is intentionally generic."""

def _check(node, depth=0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionRejected("expression too complex")
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _check(node.body, depth + 1)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — exclude it explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionRejected("unsupported literal")
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in _BIN_OPS:
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            # bound the exponent to prevent 9**9**9 style DoS
            if not (isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant)
                    and abs(node.right.value) <= MAX_EXPONENT):
                raise ExpressionRejected("exponent out of range")
        _check(node.left, depth + 1)
        _check(node.right, depth + 1)
        return
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in _UNARY_OPS:
        _check(node.operand, depth + 1)
        return
    # Name, Call, Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, JoinedStr, comprehensions → all rejected
    raise ExpressionRejected("unsupported construct")

def validate(expr: str) -> ast.Expression:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or not expr or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionRejected("invalid input")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError:
        raise ExpressionRejected("invalid syntax") from None
    _check(tree)
    return tree

def _eval(node):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body)
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        return _BIN_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.left), _eval(node.right))
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        return _UNARY_OPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
    raise ExpressionRejected("unsupported construct")

def safe_eval(expr: str) -> float:
    result = _eval(validate(expr))
    if abs(result) > 1e308:          # guard against runaway magnitude
        raise ExpressionRejected("result out of range")
    return result
```

Why `ast.parse` and not a regex: the parser gives you a real tree, so you are allowlisting **structure** rather than pattern-matching text. Regex filters are bypassable (`＿_import__` with a full-width underscore, unicode digits, `getattr` chains, etc.).

---

## 2. OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Risk | How it shows up here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** (primary) | `eval`/`exec` on user input → RCE | AST allowlist above; no `eval` anywhere in the request path |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No length/depth/exponent bounds → CPU/memory DoS | `MAX_LEN`, `MAX_DEPTH`, `MAX_EXPONENT`, result magnitude check |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Debug tracebacks or raw input echoed to the client | Generic `ExpressionRejected` message; full detail only in server logs |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Third-party expression libs with CVEs | Pin + audit; prefer the ~40-line allowlist over a general-purpose evaluator |
| **A09 Logging & Monitoring Failures** | Rejections invisible → no detection | Structured log on every rejection (see below) |

---

## 3. Error handling and logging

```python
import logging, hashlib
log = logging.getLogger("expr.security")

def handle(raw: str):
    try:
        return {"ok": True, "result": safe_eval(raw)}
    except ExpressionRejected:
        log.warning("expr.rejected", extra={
            "input_len": len(raw),
            "input_sha256": hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16],
            "client_ip": client_ip,
        })
        return {"ok": False, "error": "Invalid expression"}   # no echo, no detail
```

Log a **hash plus length**, not the raw payload — you keep detection and correlation without writing attacker-controlled strings (or PII) into your log store, which is itself an injection/log-forging vector.

---

## 4. Test cases to add to CI

```python
MALICIOUS = [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "eval('1+1')",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "9**9**9",                      # DoS
    "1+" * 5000 + "1",              # depth/length DoS
    "x",                            # Name
    "[i for i in range(10**9)]",    # comprehension
    "f'{__import__(\"os\")}'",      # f-string
    "True + 1",                     # bool literal
]
# every one must raise ExpressionRejected, and the API must return a generic 400
```

Also assert the happy path stays intact: `(1+2)*3/4 == 2.25`, `2+3*4 == 14`.

---

## 5. Checklist

- [ ] `grep -rn "eval(\|exec(\|compile(" src/` returns nothing on the request path
- [ ] Validation runs **before** any arithmetic, on the server, never trusting client-side checks
- [ ] Length, depth, exponent, and result-magnitude limits all enforced
- [ ] `bool` excluded from numeric literals; only `int`/`float` accepted
- [ ] Rejections return a generic message; details go to logs only
- [ ] Rejection events are logged with hash + length and alerted on
- [ ] Dependency audit (`pip-audit`) in CI for any parsing library you add
- [ ] Fuzz the validator (Hypothesis) with random unicode and deep nesting

The generated OWASP/NIST hardening prompt from the security tooling reinforces the same priorities — its top Python-specific check is exactly *"Check for `eval()`, `exec()`, or `compile()` on user input"*, and its input-validation guidance is *"implement whitelist validation, not just blacklist filtering"*, which is what the AST allowlist does.

## Short answer

Use **syntax validation as a cheap pre-filter, never as the security boundary**. The real control is a **positive allowlist over the parsed AST** (numbers + a fixed operator set), plus resource limits, plus OWASP-aligned logging and error handling.

I verified this distinction against your own tooling below — the validator happily accepts `eval("1+1")`.

---

## 1. What I verified empirically

| Probe | Validator result | Interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3 * 4) / 7 - 1.5` | ✅ valid → evaluated to **0.5** | Ordinary arithmetic works |
| `abs(-3) + round(2.5)` | ✅ **valid** | **Function calls / bare identifiers are accepted** |
| `eval("1+1")` | ✅ **valid** | ⚠️ **Syntax validity ≠ safety** |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected (`_`) | Blocklist catches *this* spelling only |
| `os.system("ls")` | ❌ rejected (`.` at col 3) | Attribute access blocked |
| `[x for x in (1,2)]` | ❌ rejected | Comprehensions blocked |
| `9**2`, `9**9**9` | ❌ rejected (col 3) | `**` is **not** in this grammar |
| `1/0` | ⚠️ **no error**, `result: null` | Runtime errors are swallowed |

**Three takeaways:**

1. **`eval("1+1")` validates.** Any allowlist that checks *characters* or *syntax* will pass hostile calls — because a call is syntactically legitimate. Rejecting `__import__` by name is a blocklist (defeatable via `getattr`, unicode, or rebuilt strings).
2. **`1/0` returned `null` without raising.** Do not treat "no exception" as "valid math."
3. `**` is rejected here, but **do not infer that exponentiation is safe elsewhere** — the exponent bomb is a real availability risk in most parsers.

---

## 2. The three layers

| Layer | Purpose | Never rely on it for |
|---|---|---|
| L1 Syntax/grammar check | Fast reject, cheap DoS guard | Authorization, sandboxing |
| L2 **AST allowlist** | **The actual security boundary** | — |
| L3 Resource bounds + logging | Availability, forensics | Correctness |

---

## 3. Reference implementation

```python
import ast
import math
import operator

MAX_LEN = 256          # characters of input
MAX_DEPTH = 32         # AST nesting
MAX_INT_BITS = 4096    # ~1233 decimal digits

_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,
    ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv,
    ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    # ast.Pow: operator.pow,   # enable ONLY with the exponent cap below
}


class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Returned to callers as a generic message; details go to logs."""


def _check_magnitude(value):
    if isinstance(value, int):
        if value.bit_length() > MAX_INT_BITS:
            raise ExpressionError("result too large")
        return value
    if isinstance(value, float):
        if not math.isfinite(value):        # catches 1e300 * 1e300 -> inf
            raise ExpressionError("result not finite")
        return value
    raise ExpressionError("unsupported result type")


def _depth(node, level=0):
    if level > MAX_DEPTH:
        return level
    return max((_depth(c, level + 1) for c in ast.iter_child_nodes(node)), default=level)


def _eval(node):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int -> must be excluded explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("only numeric literals allowed")
        return node.value

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and isinstance(node.op, (ast.UAdd, ast.USub)):
        val = _eval(node.operand)
        return -val if isinstance(node.op, ast.USub) else +val

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BINOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            raise ExpressionError("operator not allowed")
        left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            raise ExpressionError("division by zero")
        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):           # if you enable ast.Pow
            if not isinstance(right, (int, float)) or abs(right) > 64:
                raise ExpressionError("exponent too large")
        return _check_magnitude(op(left, right))

    # Calls, attributes, Subscript, Lambda, JoinedStr, Name, Container -> all denied
    raise ExpressionError("unsupported expression element")


def evaluate_expression(expr: str):
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionError("invalid expression")
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError):
        raise ExpressionError("invalid expression") from None
    if _depth(tree) > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too complex")
    return _check_magnitude(_eval(tree.body))
```

**Why not `ast.literal_eval`?** It rejects arithmetic (`1+1`), so it can't back a calculator — and it still permits containers/strings/complex. It is not a substitute for the walker above.

**Why not `eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})`?** The builtins-dict trick is escaped routinely via attribute chains:

```python
().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()   # -> arbitrary class access -> RCE
```
That is why attribute access (`ast.Attribute`) must be *denied*, not filtered.

---

## 4. Limits to enforce (availability is a security property)

| Limit | Suggested | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Input length | 256 chars | Cheap pre-parse rejection |
| AST depth | 32 | Prevents parser/evaluator stack exhaustion |
| Operator count | 64 | Bounds CPU per request |
| Exponent | √ | `9**9**9` is ~4×10⁸ digits — pure DoS |
| Int bit length | 4096 | Same class of attack, via multiplication |
| Result finiteness | required | `1e300*1e300` → `inf` corrupts downstream math |
| Requests/min per identity | rate limit | Blast-radius reduction |

---

## 5. OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Risk | How it applies here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** (CWE-94/95) | Expression string becomes code if you call `eval`/`exec` | AST allowlist; no `eval` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** (CWE-770) | Unbounded exponent/magnitude → CPU + memory exhaustion | Depth,

数学表达式与安全建议 · evaluated

{
  "validate_syntax__b7e42007400a": 6,
  "security-hardening-prompt-builder__b65756357998": 1,

回答

Great — the tools gave us a complete, working picture. Here's how to lock down mathematical operations in Python, grounded in what the validator just showed.

## What the validation probe revealed

The syntax validator enforces a **whitelist grammar**, and that behavior is exactly what you want to replicate:

| Input | Result | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `(100 * 1.08) + (50 / 4) - 8` | ✅ valid → evaluates to `112.5` | Only arithmetic + parens |
| `sqrt(16) + sin(0) * 2` | ✅ valid → evaluates to `4` | Whitelisted math functions |
| `2 ** 3` | ❌ rejected | `**` power op not in grammar |
| `open('secret.txt').read()` | ❌ rejected | Attribute access / non-whitelisted call |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | `_` / dunder token disallowed |
| Chained: `unit_price * 12` | ✅ → `1350` | Pre-validated variable reuse |

The lesson: **never hand raw user input to `eval()`**. The dangerous inputs failed *before* evaluation because the grammar only permits numbers, `+ - * /`, parens, and named math funcs.

## The layered defense (recommended)

### 1. Validate first (fail fast, never execute on parse error)

```python
import ast

MAX_LEN = 256

def validate_expression(expr: str) -> ast.AST:
    if not isinstance(expr, str) or not expr.strip():
        raise ValueError("empty expression")
    if len(expr) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ValueError("expression too long")          # DoS guard
    tree = ast.parse(expr, mode="eval")                   # "eval" mode => no statements,
                                                          #   no import/assign/exec possible
    return tree
```

Using `mode="eval"` means `import`, assignment, and multi-statement payloads are structurally impossible to parse — a first layer of the whitelist.

### 2. Walk the AST against an explicit allow-list (the real control)

```python
import ast, math, operator as op

ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: op.add, ast.Sub: op.sub, ast.Mult: op.mul,
    ast.Div: op.truediv, ast.Mod: op.mod, ast.FloorDiv: op.floordiv,
    # Deliberately omit ast.Pow unless you cap exponents
}
ALLOWED_UNARY = {ast.UAdd: op.pos, ast.USub: op.neg}
ALLOWED_FUNCS = {
    "abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max, "round": round,
    "sqrt": math.sqrt, "sin": math.sin, "cos": math.cos,
}

def _eval(node):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant) and type(node.value) in (int, float):
        return node.value
    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in ALLOWED_BINOPS:
        return ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.left), _eval(node.right))
    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in ALLOWED_UNARY:
        return ALLOWED_UNARY[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if (isinstance(node.func, ast.Name)
                and node.func.id in ALLOWED_FUNCS
                and not node.keywords):
            return ALLOWED_FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a) for a in node.args])
        raise ValueError("function not permitted")
    raise ValueError(f"disallowed syntax: {type(node).__name__}")

def safe_eval(expr: str) -> float:
    return _eval(validate_expression(expr).body)
```

This rejects `Attribute` access (`.read()`), `Name` lookups outside your function map (blocks `__import__`, `open`, `globals`), comprehensions, and every statement form — the exact classes of input the validator just refused.

### 3. Avoid the tempting-but-weak shortcuts

- ⚠️ `eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}}, {})` — better than bare `eval`, but bypassable via attribute chains like `().__class__.__bases__`. Don't rely on it alone for untrusted input.
- ❌ `ast.literal_eval` — **wrong tool**: it only parses literals, rejects arithmetic like `2+2`.
- ⚠️ `sympy.sympify(expr)` — convenient but executes Python; use `parse_expr(..., transformations=standard_transformations)` with a restricted namespace, or a vetted library.
- ✅ Prefer mature sandboxes if you need more: **`simpleeval`**, **`asteval`**, or **`RestrictedPython`**.

### 4. Guard the operational edges

```python
import logging, time
log = logging.getLogger("math_engine")

def handle(expr, user):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        result = safe_eval(expr)
        if math.isnan(result) or math.isinf(result):
            raise ValueError("non-finite result")      # block NaN/Inf abuse
        return {"ok": True, "result": result}
    except (ValueError, SyntaxError, ZeroDivisionError) as e:
        log.warning("rejected expr=%r user=%s reason=%s", expr, user, e)  # OWASP A09
        return {"ok": False, "error": "invalid expression"}
    finally:
        # enforce wall-clock budget on long evaluation for DoS (A04)
        if time.monotonic() - t0 > 0.1:
            log.error("slow evaluation: %r", expr)
```

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Finding | OWASP category | Control applied |
|---|---|---|
| `eval()` on user input → RCE | **A03 Injection** | Whitelist AST validation |
| No size/time limits → DoS | **A04 Insecure Design** | Length cap, result sanity, time budget |
| Builtins reachable | **A05 Misconfiguration** | AST allow-list; disabled `__builtins__` |
| Rejections not recorded | **A09 Logging/ Monitoring Failures** | Structured warning logs |
| Ad-hoc parser drift | **A08 Data Integrity** | Deterministic, tested evaluator |

## Security test cases to add to CI

```python
import pytest
from math_engine import safe_eval

BAD = [
    "__import__('os').system('ls')",   # RCE
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",      # file read
    "2 ** 999999",                     # exponent DoS
    "().__class__.__bases__",          # sandbox escape
    "x := 5",                          # walrus/assignment
    "[i for i in range(10**9)]",       # comprehension DoS
    "1; import os",                    # multi-statement
]
GOOD = {"(100 * 1.08) + (50/4) - 8": 112.5, "sqrt(16) + sin(0)*2": 4.0}

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", BAD)
def test_rejects(expr):
    with pytest.raises((ValueError, SyntaxError)):
        safe_eval(expr)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", GOOD.items())
def test_accepts(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == pytest.approx(expected)
```

## Bottom line

1. **Validate** with a strict whitelist grammar (as the MCP validator does) — reject `**`, underscores, attribute access, and non-whitelisted calls *before* evaluating.
2. **Evaluate** only through an AST walker with an explicit operator/function allow-map — never bare `eval`.
3. **Guard** the edges: length caps, finite-number checks, time budgets, and rejection logging.
4. **Test** the attack corpus above in CI.

If you specifically need exponentiation (`**`), add `ast.Pow` but cap both operand magnitudes and exponent size to avoid the classic `9**9**9` DoS — that's the one arithmetic feature the reference grammar intentionally omitted. Want me to generate a full CodeQL/Semgrep rule set to catch raw `eval()`/`exec()` usage across your repo, or scaffold the `math_engine` module with tests?

  "security-hardening-prompt-builder__b65756357998": 1,
  "evaluate__7722fb090eee": 1

回答

## Core principle

`eval()` / `exec()` on user-supplied math is **arbitrary remote code execution**, not a parsing problem. No amount of regex blacklisting fixes it. The only durable answer is **allowlist-by-construction**: parse to an AST and interpret only the node types you explicitly permit.

I ran the validators you have available to show the difference in practice.

## What the validation harness actually showed

| Expression | Result |
|---|---|
| `2 + 3 * (4 - 1)` | ✅ valid → evaluates to `11` |
| `user_rate * hours` | ✅ valid (with `allow_variables=true`) |
| `__import__('os').system('id')` | ❌ rejected — *Unexpected character `_` at line 1, column 1* |
| `9**9**9**9` | ❌ rejected — grammar has no `**` operator |
| `(((((1 + 2)))))` with `max_depth=5` | ⚠️ **reported valid** — the depth limit was *not* enforced |

Two takeaways, and the second one matters:

1. A strict grammar kills the classic sandbox escape before it reaches an interpreter, because `_`, `.`, `(`, and quotes simply aren't in the alphabet.
2. **Syntax validation alone is necessary but not sufficient.** The depth cap did not fire, and a narrow grammar is itself a functional constraint you may not want. Do not delegate your resource limits to a validator — enforce length, depth, node count, and magnitude in *your* code.

## The pattern to implement

Parse, never compile-and-exec. Walk the tree yourself with a closed set of node types.

```python
import ast, math, operator
from decimal import Decimal

BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub, ast.Mult: operator.mul,
    ast.Div: operator.truediv, ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
}
UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}
FUNCS = {"abs": abs, "min": min, "max": max, "round": round}

MAX_LEN, MAX_DEPTH, MAX_NODES = 256, 20, 200
MAX_ABS = 10**18          # reject runaway integers before they eat memory


class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Raised for any untrusted-input rejection. Never leaks internals."""


def _depth(node, d=0):
    if d > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too deeply nested")
    for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
        _depth(child, d + 1)


def _bound(value):
    if isinstance(value, (int, float, Decimal)):
        if isinstance(value, float) and not math.isfinite(value):
            raise ExpressionError("non-finite result")
        if abs(value) > MAX_ABS:
            raise ExpressionError("result out of permitted range")
    return value


def _eval(node, env):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, env)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        v = node.value
        if isinstance(v, bool) or not isinstance(v, (int, float)):
            raise ExpressionError("only numeric literals are permitted")
        return _bound(v)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id.startswith("__") or node.id not in env:
            raise ExpressionError("unknown identifier")
        return _bound(env[node.id])

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp) and type(node.op) in BINOPS:
        left, right = _eval(node.left, env), _eval(node.right, env)
        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            raise ExpressionError("division by zero")
        return _bound(BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right))

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp) and type(node.op) in UNARYOPS:
        return _bound(UNARYOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand, env)))

    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in FUNCS:
            raise ExpressionError("function not permitted")
        if node.keywords:
            raise ExpressionError("keyword arguments not permitted")
        return _bound(FUNCS[node.func.id](*[_eval(a, env) for a in node.args]))

    # Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehension, IfExp, JoinedStr, Await, ...
    raise ExpressionError(f"construct not permitted: {type(node).__name__}")


def safe_eval(source: str, variables: dict) -> float:
    if not isinstance(source, str) or len(source) > MAX_LEN:
        raise ExpressionError("expression must be a string under the length limit")
    tree = ast.parse(source, mode="eval")      # builds AST; executes nothing
    _depth(tree)
    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        raise ExpressionError("expression too complex")
    return _eval(tree, variables)
```

The `raise ExpressionError(...)` on the fallthrough is the whole design: unknown node types fail closed.

### Things that look like fixes but are not

- **`eval(expr, {"__builtins__": {}})`** — escapable in one line via `().__class__.__base__.__subclasses__()`. The `__builtins__` key also does not reliably stay empty across nested frames.
- **Blacklisting `__`, `import`, `os`** — defeated by Unicode normalization, `getattr`, f-string nesting, and encoded payloads.
- **`sympy.sympify()`** — uses `eval` internally. Never on untrusted input.
- **`pandas.eval` / `numexpr`** — documented as *not* security boundaries.
- **`ast.literal_eval`** — safe, but evaluates literals only; it will not do your arithmetic.
- **`simpleeval` / `pyparsing` / `lark`** — reasonable building blocks, but they are parsing libraries; you still own the resource limits and the numeric policy.

If you'd rather not maintain a grammar, run the evaluator in a **separate process** with a wall-clock timeout, memory cap, and no network/filesystem access (`resource.setrlimit`, `multiprocessing` with `join(timeout)`). Sandboxing is defense-in-depth *behind* the whitelist, never a substitute for it.

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

Generated via the hardening profiler against this exact scenario — an HTTP endpoint taking expressions with variables `rate`, `hours`, `quantity` feeding invoicing:

| OWASP | Risk here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** | `eval` on request body = RCE | AST whitelist; `ast.parse(mode="eval")` only |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | Arithmetic is a trust boundary, not a UI feature | Threat-model it; fail closed on unknown nodes |
| **A05 Misconfiguration** | `assert` guards, debug tracebacks, permissive CORS | Debug off in prod; generic errors; explicit origin allowlist |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Old `simpleeval`/`sympy`/parser CVEs | Pin and scan deps; `pip-audit` in CI |
| **A08 Data Integrity** | Unvalidated results flow into invoices | Bound magnitudes; `Decimal` for money |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | Rejected payloads are your best intrusion signal | Audit-log every rejection with source IP, hash of input |
| **A10 SSRF** | Not in scope unless expressions choose data sources | Keep the evaluator free of I/O entirely |

**Corollary:** `rate / hours` with `hours=0` must be a handled error, not a 500 — an unhandled traceback is an A05/A09 finding.

## Correctness is also a security property

Floats in an invoicing path produce silent rounding drift — a data-integrity defect (A08). Do arithmetic in `decimal.Decimal` with an explicit quantize/rounding policy, and keep the whitelist but swap the operator table to `Decimal` methods.

## Attack corpus for your test suite

Assert each of these raises `ExpressionError` and logs an event:

```python
HOSTILE = [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__base__.__subclasses__()",
    "1 if open('/etc/passwd') else 2",
    "[c for c in ().__class__.__mro__]",
    "lambda: 0",
    "(lambda: 1)()",
    "'a' * 999999",
    "1e100000",
    "9**9**9**9",
    "(" * 60 + "1" + ")" * 60,
    "1_000_000",
    "0x10",
    "_＿import＿_('os')",      # homoglyph
]
```

Plus positive cases pinned to expected values, and a benchmark proving `9**9**9` cannot be reached by construction.

## Shipping checklist

- [ ] Zero `eval`/`exec`/`compile` on any path reachable from a request (`ruff` rule `S307`, `bandit` B307)
- [ ] AST whitelist with fail-closed fallthrough, as above
- [ ] Length ≤ 256, depth ≤ 20, nodes ≤ 200, magnitude bounded — **enforced in your code**, since the harness's `max_depth` demonstrably did not reject a depth-6 input
- [ ] Per-process timeout + memory rlimit for the evaluator
- [ ] Per-IP and per-token rate limiting (A04/A07) — expression endpoints are cheap to spam
- [ ] `Decimal` for monetary results
- [ ] Structured 4xx with a correlation ID; full detail only in server logs
- [ ] Audit log on every rejection, immutable and retained
- [ ] Hostile corpus in CI as a regression gate

One caveat on the tooling evidence above: the expression validator here is a narrow demonstration harness, not a drop-in production guard, and its `max_depth` parameter was not honored in the one probe I ran. Treat those results as a proof of concept for allowlisting, and implement the enforcement points in your own code.

回答

## Secure Mathematical Expression Handling in Python

I ran your validation pipeline against several representative inputs first. Here's what the tooling reported:

| Expression | Validation result | What it tells you |
|---|---|---|
| `(a + b) * 2 / (c - 1)` | ✅ syntactically valid | Normal arithmetic passes |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected — `Unexpected character '_'` | Code-injection payloads die at the **syntax gate** |
| `9**9**9` | ❌ rejected — `Expected expression` | Exponent-bomb / DoS payloads blocked |
| `sqrt(16) + log(100, 10)` | ✅ valid | Whitelisted function calls allowed |
| `2 + 3 * 4` (validate-only) | ✅ valid | Dry-run mode works without executing |
| `1 / 0` | ⚠️ `result: null` | **Silent failure** — you must handle this explicitly |

That last row is the important one: a `null` result is not an exception, so naive code will propagate `None` into your database or API response. Treat it as a hard error.

---

## 1. The core rule: never `eval()` user input

`eval()` / `exec()` / `compile()` on user-supplied strings is **OWASP A03: Injection** (and CWE-95, *Improper Neutralization of Directives in Dynamically Evaluated Code*). A single `__import__('os').system(...)` is full remote code execution. There is no sanitizer that makes `eval` safe — the only correct fix is to not use it.

## 2. Use an AST whitelist evaluator

Parse to an AST, walk it, and reject any node type you didn't explicitly allow. This is the pattern the validation tools above follow.

```python
import ast
import operator

# --- 1. Hard limits (defence in depth) ---
MAX_LENGTH = 256          # characters
MAX_NODES = 100           # AST node count
MAX_EXPONENT = 1000       # blocks 9**9**9
MAX_DEPTH = 20            # nesting depth

ALLOWED_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add, ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul, ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
ALLOWED_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}
ALLOWED_FUNCS = {"sqrt", "log", "abs", "min", "max", "round"}


class UnsafeExpression(ValueError):
    """Raised for anything that fails validation."""


def _check_depth(node, depth=0):
    if depth > MAX_DEPTH:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression nested too deeply")
    for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
        _check_depth(child, depth + 1)


def validate(expression: str) -> ast.Expression:
    """Stage 1+2: syntax and semantic validation. Never executes anything."""
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression must be a string")
    if len(expression) > MAX_LENGTH:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too long")
    if not expression.strip():
        raise UnsafeExpression("empty expression")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError as exc:
        raise UnsafeExpression(f"invalid syntax: {exc.msg}") from None

    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        raise UnsafeExpression("expression too complex")
    _check_depth(tree)

    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            continue
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            if not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)) or isinstance(node.value, bool):
                raise UnsafeExpression("only numeric literals allowed")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            if type(node.op) not in ALLOWED_BINOPS:
                raise UnsafeExpression(f"operator not allowed: {type(node.op).__name__}")
            if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
                # guard the exponent magnitude
                if isinstance(node.right, ast.Constant) and abs(node.right.value) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                    raise UnsafeExpression("exponent too large")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            if type(node.op) not in ALLOWED_UNARYOPS:
                raise UnsafeExpression("unary operator not allowed")
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in ALLOWED_FUNCS:
                raise UnsafeExpression("function not allowed")
            if node.keywords:
                raise UnsafeExpression("keyword arguments not allowed")
        elif isinstance(node, (ast.Load, ast.Name)):
            continue
        else:
            # Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, Comprehension, Import, etc.
            raise UnsafeExpression(f"node type not allowed: {type(node).__name__}")

    return tree


def evaluate(expression: str) -> float:
    """Stage 3: evaluate a tree that has already passed validation."""
    tree = validate(expression)

    def _eval(node):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
            return _eval(node.body)
        if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
            return node.value
        if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
            left, right = _eval(node.left), _eval(node.right)
            if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
                raise UnsafeExpression("division by zero")
            return ALLOWED_BINOPS[type(node.op)](left, right)
        if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
            return ALLOWED_UNARYOPS[type(node.op)](_eval(node.operand))
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
            import math
            return getattr(math, node.func.id)(*[_eval(a) for a in node.args])
        raise UnsafeExpression("unexpected node during evaluation")

    result = _eval(tree)
    if not isinstance(result, (int, float)) or result != result:  # NaN check
        raise UnsafeExpression("non-finite result")
    return result
```

**Why this is safe:** the AST never contains `Attribute`, `Subscript`, `Lambda`, `Import`, or `Name`-as-callable nodes, so `__import__`, `().__class__.__bases__`, and friends are structurally impossible — not merely filtered.

## 3. Layer the defences

| Layer | Control | OWASP |
|---|---|---|
| Syntax gate | `ast.parse` + reject `SyntaxError` | A03 Injection |
| Semantic gate | Node/operator/function whitelist | A03, A04 Insecure Design |
| Resource gate | Length, node count, depth, exponent caps | A04, DoS |
| Runtime gate | Division-by-zero, NaN/Inf checks, `signal.alarm` or `multiprocessing` timeout | A04 |
| Error handling | Return generic messages; log details server-side | A09 Logging Failures |
| Output | Cast to `float`, round, reject non-finite | A03 |

**Timeouts matter.** Even a whitelisted `9**9**9`-style expression can burn CPU. Run evaluation in a subprocess with a wall-clock limit, or use `signal.setitimer` on Unix:

```python
import signal

class Timeout(Exception): pass

def _handler(signum, frame):
    raise Timeout("evaluation timed out")

def evaluate_with_timeout(expr: str, seconds: float = 0.5) -> float:
    signal.signal(signal.SIGALRM, _handler)
    signal.setitimer(signal.ITIMER_REAL, seconds)
    try:
        return evaluate(expr)
    finally:
        signal.setitimer(signal.ITIMER_REAL, 0)
```

## 4. Error handling and logging

```python
import logging
logger = logging.getLogger("math.eval")

def safe_evaluate(expr: str) -> dict:
    try:
        value = evaluate_with_timeout(expr)
        return {"ok": True, "result": value}
    except UnsafeExpression as exc:
        logger.warning("rejected expression", extra={"expr": expr[:256], "reason": str(exc)})
        return {"ok": False, "error": "invalid expression"}   # generic to the client
    except Timeout:
        logger.warning("expression timeout", extra={"expr": expr[:256]})
        return {"ok": False, "error": "expression too complex"}
    except Exception:
        logger.exception("unexpected evaluation failure")     # full trace server-side only
        return {"ok": False, "error": "internal error"}
```

Never return `str(exc)` or a traceback to the caller — that's an information-disclosure finding under **A05 Security Misconfiguration** / **A09**.

## 5. Dependency and CI security

```bash
pip-audit                 # CVE scan of installed packages (A06)
bandit -r src/ -ll        # flags eval/exec/pickle/subprocess (A03)
ruff check --select S     # flake8-bandit rules
semgrep --config p/python # injection patterns
```

Add a CI gate that fails on `bandit -ll` findings of severity ≥ Medium, and pin dependencies with hashes (`pip-compile --generate-hashes`).

## 6. Test cases to lock the behaviour in

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("payload", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "().__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "9**9**9",
    "eval('1+1')",
    "x = 1",
    "[i for i in range(10**9)]",
    "lambda: 1",
    "a.b.c",
])
def test_rejects_dangerous(payload):
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        evaluate(payload)

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("2 + 3 * 4", 14),
    ("(1 + 2) / 4", 0.75),
    ("sqrt(16)", 4.0),
    ("-5 + 2", -3),
])
def test_accepts_arithmetic(expr, expected):
    assert evaluate(expr) == pytest.approx(expected)

def test_division_by_zero():
    with pytest.raises(UnsafeExpression):
        evaluate("1 / 0")
```

## 7. OWASP Top 10 mapping for this specific risk

- **A03 Injection** — the primary risk; solved by AST whitelisting, not `eval`.
- **A04 Insecure Design** — resource limits, timeouts, and fail-closed defaults are design controls, not patches.
- **A05 Security Misconfiguration** — generic error messages, no debug tracebacks in responses.
- **A06 Vulnerable & Outdated Components** — `pip-audit` in CI; the parser library itself is attack surface.
- **A08 Software & Data Integrity** — pin and hash dependencies; verify lockfiles.
- **A09 Logging & Monitoring Failures** — log every rejection with the payload and reason; alert on spikes (a burst of `__import__` attempts is an active probe).

---

### Generated assessment prompt

I also produced a full OWASP-aligned hardening prompt via the security builder. Its scope covers **input validation, error handling, logging/monitoring, configuration security, API security, and dependency security**, with Python-specific checks for `eval`/`exec`/`compile`, `pickle.loads`, `os.system`/`subprocess` shell injection, and path traversal — plus a risk matrix (Likelihood × Impact) and before/after code examples. Use it as the review checklist for the module above.

**Bottom line:** validate syntax → whitelist AST nodes → cap resources → timeout → sanitize errors → log rejections → audit dependencies. The `1 / 0 → null` result you saw is the one gap the validator won't catch for you; make it an explicit exception in your own code.

## What the validation layer actually caught

I ran the validator against a set of expressions to establish a real baseline rather than assume one.

| Expression | Verdict | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `(2 + 3) * 4 / 2 - 1` | ✅ valid → `9` | Correct precedence |
| `sqrt(144) + abs(-7) + min(3, 9)` | ✅ valid → `22` | Function calls resolve |
| `x + 2 * y` (vars allowed) | ✅ valid | Variable mode works |
| `__import__('os').system('rm -rf /')` | ❌ rejected | `Unexpected character '_'` |
| `(1).__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()` | ❌ rejected | Same lexical rule |
| `[x for x in range(10)]` | ❌ rejected | `Expected expression` |
| `lambda: 1` | ❌ rejected | `Expected ']' after array elements` |
| `9**9**9**9` | ⚠️ **valid** | **Not caught** |
| `1 / 0` | ⚠️ **`result: null`, no error** | **Silent failure** |

Three findings drive the design below:

1. **The `__import__` rejection is accidental, not designed.** It failed because `_` isn't in the lexer's character set — not because attribute access or imports are on a denylist. Any expression avoiding underscores reaches the evaluator. Denylists fail open; allowlists fail closed.
2. **`9**9**9**9` passes syntax validation.** Syntax validity says nothing about resource cost. Right-associative exponentiation is a one-line memory-exhaustion DoS, and no amount of grammar checking will catch it.
3. **`1 / 0` returned `null` with `type: "number"`.** A silent null is worse than an exception: downstream code that checks `if result is not None` will happily persist a null into a report, and nothing gets logged. This is an OWASP A09 (Logging & Monitoring Failures) issue as much as a correctness bug.

Also worth noting: `max_depth` did not behave as a reliable control — `2 + 3 * 4` passed with `max_depth=1`, and deeply nested parens failed with a generic `Expected expression` rather than a depth violation. Don't rely on it as your DoS guard. (`batch_evaluate` errored out with an internal handler fault, so I evaluated individually.)

## The secure pattern: AST allowlist, not string filtering

The only defensible approach is to parse to an AST and walk it, permitting an explicit set of node types. Never `eval()`.

```python
import ast
import operator
from decimal import Decimal, DivisionByZero, InvalidOperation

class ExpressionError(ValueError):
    """Internal detail. Never return str(e) to the client."""

MAX_LENGTH   = 512
MAX_NODES    = 100
MAX_EXPONENT = 64
MAX_ABS      = Decimal("1e12")

_BINOPS = {
    ast.Add: operator.add,       ast.Sub: operator.sub,
    ast.Mult: operator.mul,      ast.Div: operator.truediv,
    ast.FloorDiv: operator.floordiv, ast.Mod: operator.mod,
    ast.Pow: operator.pow,
}
_UNARYOPS = {ast.UAdd: operator.pos, ast.USub: operator.neg}
_FUNCS = {
    "sqrt": lambda x: x.sqrt() if x >= 0 else _fail("sqrt of negative"),
    "abs":  abs,
    "min":  min,
    "max":  max,
    "round": lambda x, n=0: round(x, int(n)),
}

def _fail(msg):
    raise ExpressionError(msg)

def _guard_magnitude(value):
    if not value.is_finite() or abs(value) > MAX_ABS:
        _fail("result out of range")
    return value

def _eval(node, variables):
    if isinstance(node, ast.Expression):
        return _eval(node.body, variables)

    if isinstance(node, ast.Constant):
        # bool is a subclass of int — reject it explicitly
        if isinstance(node.value, bool) or not isinstance(node.value, (int, float)):
            _fail("only numeric literals allowed")
        return _guard_magnitude(Decimal(str(node.value)))

    if isinstance(node, ast.Name):
        if node.id not in variables:          # explicit allowlist, no fallback
            _fail("unknown identifier")
        return _guard_magnitude(Decimal(str(variables[node.id])))

    if isinstance(node, ast.UnaryOp):
        op = _UNARYOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            _fail("unary operator not allowed")
        return _guard_magnitude(op(_eval(node.operand, variables)))

    if isinstance(node, ast.BinOp):
        op = _BINOPS.get(type(node.op))
        if op is None:
            _fail("operator not allowed")
        left  = _eval(node.left, variables)
        right = _eval(node.right, variables)

        if isinstance(node.op, ast.Pow):
            # integral exponent only, tightly bounded
            if right != right.to_integral_value() or abs(right) > MAX_EXPONENT:
                _fail("exponent out of range")
            if abs(left) > 1 and right > MAX_EXPONENT:
                _fail("exponent out of range")

        if isinstance(node.op, (ast.Div, ast.FloorDiv, ast.Mod)) and right == 0:
            _fail("division by zero")          # explicit, never silent

        try:
            return _guard_magnitude(op(left, right))
        except (DivisionByZero, InvalidOperation, OverflowError):
            _fail("arithmetic error")

    if isinstance(node, ast.Call):
        if not isinstance(node.func, ast.Name) or node.func.id not in _FUNCS:
            _fail("function not allowed")
        if node.keywords or any(isinstance(a, ast.Starred) for a in node.args):
            _fail("unsupported call form")
        args = [_eval(a, variables) for a in node.args]
        return _guard_magnitude(Decimal(str(_FUNCS[node.func.id](*args))))

    # Everything else — Attribute, Subscript, Lambda, comprehension,
    # JoinedStr, Await, NamedExpr, Compare, BoolOp — is rejected here.
    _fail(f"node type not allowed: {type(node).__name__}")


def safe_eval(expression: str, variables: dict | None = None) -> Decimal:
    if not isinstance(expression, str):
        _fail("expression must be a string")
    if len(expression) > MAX_LENGTH:
        _fail("expression too long")

    try:
        tree = ast.parse(expression, mode="eval")
    except SyntaxError:
        _fail("malformed expression")

    if sum(1 for _ in ast.walk(tree)) > MAX_NODES:
        _fail("expression too complex")

    return _eval(tree, variables or {})
```

Why each piece matters:

- **`mode="eval"`** — rejects statements, assignments, and imports at the parser level.
- **`ast.Constant` with a `bool` check** — `True` is an `int` in Python; without this, `True + True` silently yields `2`.
- **`Decimal(str(...))`** — avoids binary float drift in financial math, and `str()` round-tripping prevents `Decimal(0.1)` → `0.1000000000000000055511151231257827`.
- **Explicit `Name` allowlist** — no `__builtins__` fallback, so `open`, `eval`, `getattr` are unreachable even if the AST walker is bypassed.
- **`_fail` on division by zero** — the exact bug the reference tool exhibited.
- **Node count + length caps** — the `9**9**9**9` class of attack.

## OWASP Top 10 mapping

| Risk | Manifestation here | Control |
|---|---|---|
| **A03 Injection** (CWE-95) | `eval()` on user input → RCE | AST allowlist; no `eval`/`exec`/`compile` |
| **A04 Insecure Design** | No resource bounds; `9**9**9**9` | Length, node-count, exponent, magnitude caps |
| **A05 Security Misconfiguration** | Tracebacks returned to client | Generic client message; detail server-side only |
| **A09 Logging Failures** | `1/0` → silent `null` | Raise + log with correlation ID |
| **A06 Vulnerable Components** | Parser/eval library CVEs | Pin versions, `pip-audit` in CI |
| **A01 Broken Access Control** | Unauthenticated calculator endpoint | Auth + per-user rate limiting |

## Test cases to lock the behavior in

```python
import pytest

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr,expected", [
    ("2 + 3 * 4", Decimal("14")),
    ("(2 + 3) * 4", Decimal("20")),
    ("2 ** 10", Decimal("1024")),
    ("sqrt(144)", Decimal("12")),
    ("-3 + 1", Decimal("-2")),
])
def test_valid(expr, expected):
    assert safe_eval(expr) == expected

@pytest.mark.parametrize("expr", [
    "__import__('os').system('id')",
    "(1).__class__.__bases__[0].__subclasses__()",
    "open('/etc/passwd').read()",
    "[x for x in range(10)]",
    "lambda: 1",
    "x if y else z",
    "9**9**9**9",
    "2 ** 1000",
    "1 / 0",
    "1 % 0",
    "sqrt(-1)",
    "a" * 600,
    "1 + " * 200 + "1",
    "True + True",
])
def test_rejected(expr):
    with pytest.raises(ExpressionError):
        safe_eval(expr)
```
